- **[CurveBench: A Benchmark for Exact Topological Reasoning over Nested Jordan Curves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.14068)**  `arXiv:2605.14068`  `cs.CV` `cs.LG`  
  _Amirreza Mohseni, Mona Mohammadi, Morteza Saghafian, Naser Talebizadeh Sardari_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We introduce CurveBench, a benchmark for visual topological reasoning. Given an image of disjoint closed curves, a model must recover the tree of adjacent regions rooted at the exterior. The benchmark contains 756 hand-drawn images, ranging from simple configurations to maze-like and densely packed arrangements. Each image has a manually verified reference tree. We evaluate predictions by exact tree matching with the exterior root fixed. Gemini 3.1 Pro, the strongest model in our evaluation, achieves 71.1% exact-match accuracy on CurveBench-Easy and 28.7% on CurveBench-Hard. A Qwen3-VL-8B checkpoint trained using reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards reaches 31.7% on Easy, compared with 21.7% for its Thinking base model at a 16,384-token generation budget. The same comparison yields a gain of 1.1 percentage points on Hard.
  </details>

- **[Logit-Conditioned Diffusion Decoding for Frozen Discrete-Token VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.13389)**  `arXiv:2603.13389`  `cs.CV` `cs.LG`  
  _Ji Woo Hong, Gwanhyeong Koo, Hee Suk Yoon, Eunseop Yoon, SooHwan Eom, Qi Dai, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pre-trained discrete-token VLMs generate images by sampling sequences of codebook indices, but their visual fidelity is bounded by the VQ-VAE decoding stage. Prior work addresses this bottleneck by replacing the discrete tokenizer with continuous or hybrid visual token representations, or by integrating diffusion into the generation pipeline; both require foundation-scale VLM retraining or realignment. We show this is unnecessary: before each token is sampled, discrete-token VLMs already compute a full logit distribution over the codebook, but standard decoding discards it after selecting a single index. Under Otsu's threshold, the pre-sampling logits of capable VLMs assign statistically meaningful probability to at least two codebook entries per token, and recovering this distribution can substantially close the fidelity gap. We propose DCDD, a post-hoc framework that conditions a diffusion decoder on this discarded distribution while leaving the VLM frozen. At inference, DCDD converts the VLM's pre-sampling logits into distribution-weighted code vectors; during training, it uses VQ-VAE encoder-derived proxy logits calibrated to match the distributional statistics of the VLM's inference-time logits. The decoder maps these representations to high-fidelity images as an alternative to the native VQ-VAE decoder. Trained on ImageNet-1K for 50K steps, DCDD reduces reconstruction FID by 74% and Janus-Pro 7B's generation FID on MJHQ-30K by 34%, while semantic alignment across GenEval, DPG-Bench, and WISE remain consistent. Controlled comparisons with diffusion models from the same backbone family, applied either as an image-to-image refiner or as an identically trained decoder on the sampled codes, confirm that the gain stems from conditioning on the VLM's pre-sampling logit distribution rather than from the diffusion model itself.
  </details>

- **[Counting Events, Not Seeing Them: Diagnosing and Repairing the Readout Failure in Video LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09949)**  `arXiv:2611.09949`  `cs.CV` `cs.CL` `cs.LG`  
  _Dongwu Qian_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video LLMs fail conspicuously at counting how many times an object crosses the screen, a failure attributed to missing object permanence. Using construction-grounded synthetic testbeds with independently validated labels, we show this attribution is incomplete: the count signal is present but unread - linear probes decode per-window presence at 93% while global count decoding collapses to a duration confound. Concurrent work reports the same representation-output dissociation for repetition counting; we show that in exit-and-reappear event counting it is repairable: two readout-directed repairs (episode-localization supervision and a discrete event-symbol side channel) raise same-object counting from 46.7% to 65.0% (oracle 66.7%) on a chance-33% benchmark across 7B-30B systems. Transfer to an independent factory costs nothing, and an audit of the measurement apparatus exposes four data-integrity failures that had obscured the boundary. A real-video audit (REALCOUNT, n=18, blind-audited) shows the boundary persists - best 55.6% at chance 33.3% - and symbol transfer fails zero-shot. A 1-epoch in-domain adaptation bench is negative (six levers at 28-50%), but a 3-epoch schedule shows this is budget-limited for the symbol channel: with a 3-epoch schedule and sequence-disjoint oracle timelines the reader reaches 94.4% on REALCOUNT, and with the deployed detector's timelines a 2-epoch run reaches 72.2%. The residual gap is detector evidence and the reader's match to detector noise, not reader capacity: a frozen-feature boundary probe finds the signal weak (AUC 0.62 over a 0.51 permutation control) and non-transferring across clips (0.77 within-clip vs. 0.62 across windows).
  </details>

- **[Scene-Adaptive Readout and Decoding in Diffusion Driving VLAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02076)**  `arXiv:2611.02076`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.RO`  
  _Seungjun Yu, Jiho Choi, Inha Kang, Hojun Choi, Seungwook Kim, Hyunjung Shim_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion driving vision-language-action models repeatedly execute the backbone at every denoising stage, incurring substantial inference cost, while it remains unclear whether all scenes require the same decoding computation. To distinguish when a trajectory becomes readable from how much further refinement it requires, we analyze trajectory readout across network depth and decoding progress. We identify a depth-decoding dissociation in which late-layer representations already support useful trajectory readout after the first forward sweep, whereas early-layer representations benefit more from continued decoding. We further find that both the preferred readout layer and decoding depth vary across scenes. Based on these observations, we propose a scene-adaptive readout and decoding framework. A scene-conditioned readout MoE adaptively weights and combines trajectory predictions from layer-specific late-layer representations after a single backbone sweep, while an adaptive decoding policy applies additional native refinement only when required by the scene. We evaluate our framework on two diffusion driving VLAs across the Waymo E2E and nuScenes benchmarks. Our method improves validation RFS by up to 0.56 while also improving test performance, and achieves an end-to-end speedup of up to $6.0\times$ on Waymo E2E, while consistently reducing trajectory error on nuScenes. These results demonstrate that scene-level readout and decoding allocation improves planning quality while avoiding unnecessary decoding. Our code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[Split and Bridge: Multimodal Generation via Diffusion Bridging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10441)**  `arXiv:2611.10441`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.LG`  
  _Ahmad Arrabi, Xiaohan Zhang, Xingyu Li, Safwan Wshah_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual data are often expressed through multiple complementary modalities (e.g., images and segmentation masks), each capturing different aspects of the same underlying structure. Most existing multimodal methods adopt a top-down paradigm, learning a shared latent variable through joint training from scratch, which limits scalability. We propose Split and Bridge (SnB), a bottom-up framework for multimodal generation that couples pretrained unimodal diffusion models at sampling time. Rather than learning a unified latent space, SnB generates coherent multimodal samples by following a shared diffusion trajectory up to a split point and then branching into modality-specific trajectories guided by a bridge module. This design enables plug-and-play reuse of powerful pretrained models without joint training, making it scalable and easily adaptable. Across standard benchmarks (PolyMNIST, CelebAMask-HQ) and more complex settings (PIE-Bench++, ROCO), SnB achieves a strong balance between sample quality and coherence. We show that SnB naturally extends to downstream zero-shot tasks such as interleaved co-generation, image editing, and style coupling/decoupling. Our results demonstrate that bottom-up generation provides a practical framework that complements existing top-down multimodal approaches. Code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[SurvAD: How Anomalies Survive Diffusion Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09997)**  `arXiv:2611.09997`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.LG`  
  _Lexing Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Diffusion-based anomaly detectors reconstruct a test image towards normality, yet how much of an anomaly survives, its leak, remains uncharacterized. We show that a first-order deterministic sampler obeys an exact recursion along the anomaly, whose continuous limit is a survival law. The anomaly decays exponentially as the noise falls, at a rate set by the retention $r$, the share the model keeps on the states it visits. Because $r$ depends on the noise level and not on the step count, one measured path predicts the leak of other step counts, samplers and schedules from a nearby start to within a few hundredths, on four diffusion models and real defects. The law also shows that the leak's growth over steps is exposure bias and that normal details obey the same law, so changing the path or the training trades leak for detail error. We introduce SurvAD, which fine-tunes the reconstructor on inference-like starts and fits a comparator that adds evidence to the residual map without suppressing its ranking elsewhere. For under $2\%$ more inference time, SurvAD raises the multi-class pixel average precision (P-AP) of DeCo-Diff from $76.8$ to $80.7$ on MVTec AD and from $57.5$ to $60.0$ on VisA. It matches CostFilter-AD on the same reconstructor with $9\times$ fewer trainable parameters and, under Dinomaly's protocol, exceeds its reported P-AP on Dinomaly on both benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[Partition-Aware Unlearning for Removing Spurious Correlations in Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.29996)**  `arXiv:2608.29996`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.LG`  
  _Aditi Sarker, Nazreen Shah, Rafi Ibn Sultan, Rhongho Jang, Dongxiao Zhu, Prashant Khanduri_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) achieve strong performance across many multimodal tasks; however, they often exploit spurious object-background correlations, resulting in predictions driven by contextual shortcuts rather than object-relevant visual evidence. Despite growing interest in hallucination and robustness evaluation, existing benchmarks provide limited control over whether model predictions are grounded in the target object or induced by correlated background cues. In this work, we introduce PURGE (\underline{P}artition-aware \underline{U}nlearning for \underline{R}emoving spurious-correlation \underline{G}enerated \underline{E}rrors), a framework for constructing, benchmarking, and mitigating spurious-correlation-induced failures in LVLMs. The framework consists of: -- (1) Structured dataset construction wherein we develop three complementary structured data construction strategies that partition examples by object-relevant evidence and spurious background cues, enabling controlled diagnosis of shortcut reliance; and -- (2) Partition-aware unlearning, which uses these partitions to selectively remove spurious object-background associations while preserving object-based reasoning. We evaluate the \algo~framework across multiple LVLMs, including LLaVA-1.6-7B, Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct, and Qwen3.5-9B, together with CLIP as a vision-language encoder, on a diverse suite of benchmarks, including CHAIR, POPE, Causal-HalBench, MM-SpuBench, AMBER, MMHal, and Waterbirds. Our results show that PURGE consistently reduces hallucinations and spurious-correlation-driven errors while maintaining or improving overall performance in most evaluated settings, providing both a reusable evaluation protocol and an effective mitigation framework for more reliable LVLMs.
  </details>

- **[LAION-BVD: A 13-Million-Hour Open Video Dataset for Multimodal Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.24845)**  `arXiv:2608.24845`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.LG`  
  _Andreas Hochlehnert, Marianna Nezhurina, Mehdi Cherti, Andrej Radonjic, Thaddäus Wiedemer, Christoph Schuhmann, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We present LAION-BVD (LAION - Big Video Dataset), a large-scale open video dataset for multimodal learning, which contains 1.3B platform-specific video URLs collected from CommonCrawl. From these, we download 80M videos with a total duration of 13.5 million hours. The dataset is designed for multimodal pre-training across the video, audio, and image modalities. Using content-aware scene detection, we extract clips for which we synthetically generate video and audio captions. Models trained on these data achieve competitive performance on standard video-text and audio-text benchmarks, with consistent improvements as training or model scale increases. Additionally, we explore video frames as an alternative source of image-text data by extracting scene-changing frames. These frames exhibit a visual distribution distinct from standard web image corpora, and models trained on this dataset achieve strong image-text retrieval performance. We release LAION-BVD to the research community. It significantly expands open access to multimodal videos at an unprecedented scale.
  </details>

- **[HumanSightBench: A Benchmark for Human Visual Perception in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12305)**  `arXiv:2611.12305`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.CL`  
  _Zixin Su, Yangguang Lin, Zegang Peng, Yuntong Sun, Xuanyi Wang, Jiahao Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have demonstrated strong performance on knowledge-intensive and reasoning-based vision-language benchmark datasets, but still struggle to solve the visual questions that people can solve easily. Evaluating the differences between MLLMs and human-level visual perception is challenging due to end-to-end evaluation which combines various skills, or because we are uncertain if individual items are valid. Specifically, observed errors may reflect visual perception, language priors, background knowledge, question ambiguity, and underspecified answers. Our guideline for HumanSightBench is that we construct items that are human-easy by construction and model-hard by evaluation. HumanSightBench contains 2,876 visual questions with short answers across 7 top-level perceptual categories and 21 subcategories. We only include items that pass five inclusion gates: question clarity, answer uniqueness, visual grounding, human solvability, and text-only shortcut filtering. According to the protocol, errors are defined as incorrect answers on human-validated visual tasks with the evaluated interface. The inclusion gates are intended to reduce confounds from ambiguity and external knowledge. The best pass@3 score is 65.6% across eight evaluated MLLMs. No evaluated model exceeds 27.5% on Counting. These results identify capability gaps that are hidden by overall scores and can help future training experiments and held-out evaluation.
  </details>

- **[ClinFusion: A Vision-Centric Multimodal LLM System for Holistic Medical Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.24743)**  `arXiv:2607.24743`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.CL`  
  _Hangjie Yuan, Yichen Qian, Zhiwei Tang, Xianzhe Xu, Sicheng Yang, Lirong Wu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) hold immense potential to revolutionize clinical practice, yet deploying them in the medical domain is fundamentally a vision-centric challenge: models must absorb knowledge from heterogeneous 2D and 3D medical images, and evaluation protocols must align with radiologists' clinical practice and provide an accurate, fine-grained and factualness-driven assessment. In this paper, we introduce ClinFusion, a vision-centric MLLM designed for holistic medical understanding that systematically addresses these limitations. We propose a compositional and cascaded vision encoder architecture featuring a Cascade Spatial-Aware Locality Fusion operator that unifies diverse 2D and native 3D medical image understanding within a fused encoder. We further introduce a vision-grounded evaluation framework, including MedIF-Bench for instruction-following assessment and a region-of-interest-grounded method for clinically aligned and factualness-driven report generation evaluation. We show that ClinFusion sets a new state-of-the-art across a comprehensive suite of 2D and 3D multimodal medical benchmarks---spanning visual question answering, report generation, and instruction following---as well as textual medical tasks, outperforming leading open-source medical MLLMs (\textit{e.g.}, Hulu-Med, Lingshu) on 20 out of 24 benchmarks and demonstrating multimodal capabilities better than powerful proprietary models such as GPT-5.2 and Gemini-3-Flash on 13 out of 16 benchmarks, and can be further augmented with agentic tool use for retrieval-augmented and tool-assisted clinical workflows. A blinded evaluation by board-certified radiologists confirms that ClinFusion produces the highest-ranked reports, and validates our RoI-grounded metric as achieving the strongest correlation with expert judgment among all automatic evaluation metrics examined.
  </details>

- **[Animation2Code: Evaluating Temporal Visual Reasoning in Video-to-Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.28593)**  `arXiv:2606.28593`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.CL`  
  _Anya Ji, Abhijith Varma Mudunuri, David M. Chan, Alane Suhr_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  While recent vision-language models (VLMs) have achieved significant improvements on static visual-to-code tasks such as generating code for webpages, charts, or SVGs, it remains unclear whether they can recover temporal dynamics when motion is present. To this end, we introduce Animation2Code, a benchmark for evaluating temporal visual reasoning via reconstructing executable web animation code from videos. Animation2Code consists of 1,069 web animation videos with diverse visual appearances and motion patterns, paired with corresponding HTML/CSS/JavaScript implementations. We propose two human-aligned metrics, appearance similarity and temporal similarity, which allow us to disentangle visual fidelity from temporal alignment when comparing rendered animations against ground-truth samples. Benchmarking state-of-the-art VLMs on this dataset shows that current VLMs struggle to maintain temporal consistency in reconstruction, even when achieving high appearance similarity, including under finetuning and iterative refinement settings. Code and data are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[ChartHal: A Fine-grained Framework Evaluating Hallucination of Large Vision Language Models in Chart Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.17481)**  `arXiv:2509.17481`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.CL`  
  _Xingqi Wang, Yiming Cui, Xin Yao, Shijin Wang, Guoping Hu, Xiaoyu Qin_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) have recently demonstrated remarkable progress, yet hallucination remains a critical barrier, particularly in chart understanding, which requires sophisticated perceptual and cognitive abilities as well as rigorous factual accuracy. While prior work has investigated hallucinations and chart comprehension independently, their intersection remains largely unexplored. To address this gap, we present ChartHal, a benchmark that features a fine-grained taxonomy of hallucination scenarios in chart understanding, along with a human-validated dataset of 1,062 samples. Our evaluation shows that state-of-the-art LVLMs suffer from severe hallucinations on ChartHal, including proprietary models such as GPT-5 and o4-mini, which achieve only 34.46% and 22.79% accuracy, respectively. Further analysis reveals that questions involving information absent from or contradictory to charts are especially likely to trigger hallucinations, underscoring the urgent need for more robust mitigation strategies. Code and data are available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[2D Gum-Net: Unsupervised Dense Elastic Image Registration via Symmetric Correlation and Spectral Pooling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12805)**  `arXiv:2611.12805`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Aarya Bhave, Anushka Ajay Bhave_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Dense non-rigid registration, the recovery of a pixel-wise deformation field that warps one image into geometric agreement with another, is fundamental across medical imaging, structural biology, and correspondence estimation, yet learning-based approaches face a severe bottleneck: ground-truth deformation fields are notoriously difficult to obtain for real data. We present 2D Gum-Net, an end-to-end architecture that predicts dense elastic deformation fields fully unsupervised. We transfer the Geometric Unsupervised Matching Network (Gum-Net), originally developed for 3D subtomogram alignment in cryo-electron tomography, to 2D elastic registration: all three modules are redesigned in 2D, and the rigid transformation head is replaced by a Dense Spatial Transformer that regresses a sparse control-point grid, lifted to a full-resolution field $\Phi$ by bicubic interpolation. Downsampling uses DCT spectral pooling, implemented as frozen-basis matrix multiplications ($\approx 50\times$ GPU speedup), preserving the fine, quasi-periodic structure that max and average pooling destroy. We evaluate on contact-based fingerprints, a challenging testbed for this task: locally self-similar ridge patterns make correspondence maximally ambiguous, skin pressed against a sensor generates authentic large-displacement elastic distortion, and minutiae act as precise landmarks through which an independent matcher externally verifies the topological correctness of predicted fields. Trained purely on synthetic impressions and evaluated zero-shot on real benchmarks, our method improves structural correlation by up to 15.7% on FVC2004 and raises genuine match scores under an independent matcher while leaving imposter scores essentially unchanged. This suggests that the predicted deformations restore true structure without fabricating correspondences.
  </details>

- **[GeoScope: Training-Free Program Execution beyond the Counting Horizon for Ultra-High-Resolution Remote Sensing Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12699)**  `arXiv:2611.12699`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Guohui Deng, Yuhang Jiang, Chengjun Wang, Zhongwen Hu, Zhaocong Wu, Linsheng Huang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In ultra-high-resolution (UHR) remote sensing images, the objects that questions count in a region or relate by direction often span only a few pixels. Existing methods mainly decide where a vision-language model (VLM) should look and trust its answer in the chosen view. We show that this assumption fails for counting, and measure VLMs as one calibrates a sensor. All eight open VLMs tested fall increasingly short once a view holds more than a certain number of objects, about five for most on DOTA aerial images. This counting horizon narrows as views grow and objects appear smaller. An empirical power-law response model predicts the undercount a split recovers and the spurious counts it adds, and supports the rule of splitting only views beyond the horizon. In GeoScope, a text-only language model parses the question into a typed spatial program that fixes the region and its resolution. The VLM only grounds and counts within its operating range, and directions are computed explicitly from the grounded boxes. To our knowledge, it is the first UHR method to split views by a measured limit of the VLM. Without training, it improves Qwen3-VL-8B by 16.3 points on regional counting and 10.5 on spatial relations in XLRS-Bench, and has the highest reported accuracy, trained methods included, on regional counting and on the whole benchmark by question. On UHR-Micro it improves global and regional counting by 16.8 and 36.2 points, 15.6 of the former from splitting. On MME-RealWorld counting it gains 11.4 points, and we show that an option that is almost never correct yet often chosen distorts comparisons there. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[WanderBench: Can World Models Explore Dynamic Worlds from Video?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12557)**  `arXiv:2611.12557`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Yuhang Chen, Jiheng Li, Jiale Wang, Zai Wang, Guangyue Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video world models conditioned on an input video make it possible to explore real, recorded dynamic scenes, enabling re-shooting of footage, free-viewpoint replay, and real-to-sim environments for embodied agents. Such uses require the generated rollout to stay faithful to the recorded world, yet existing benchmarks start from a single image or a text prompt, and video-conditioned generation is evaluated mainly on perceptual quality and camera following. These evaluations do not directly assess whether exploration preserves subject identity, event progression, and scene consistency upon revisiting a location. We introduce WanderBench, a benchmark for exploring dynamic worlds from video. It comprises 745 single-shot source videos of real and AI-generated content in three difficulty tiers (3-25 s), paired with camera routes given as keyboard actions, and evaluates six dimensions with 24 human-validated sub-metrics. Benchmarking eight models, we find that as clips grow longer, models keep the static scene and visual quality but lose camera control and the source dynamics, and that none keeps revisited places consistent. We further propose WanderLand, a training-free framework that conditions each frame of a frozen model on the same-instant source and, on revisits, on its own earlier static content. WanderLand improves five models on every dimension, by up to 8.3 points in source-dynamics fidelity.
  </details>

- **[dNaViT: Discrete Codes are General-Purpose Visual Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12426)**  `arXiv:2611.12426`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Xiaotong Li, Zhengrong Yue, Xu Huang, Jialv Zou, Minhao Jing, Wenhao Yan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Discrete autoregressive modeling, notably through next-token prediction, has become a cornerstone of modern language models. Extending this paradigm to vision allows visual and language models to converge on a unified framework, thereby fostering shared methodology and infrastructure. This work investigates a central question: how far can a finite discrete vocabulary go in representing the richness of the visual world? We present dNaViT, a Discrete Native-resolution Vision Transformer that produces discrete visual primitives analogous to language tokens. Built upon a frozen pretrained encoder, dNaViT applies lightweight residual modulation with a dual-branch objective to inject reconstruction-aware cues into semantic features. By leveraging grouped product quantization, it scales discrete capacity without inflating spatial sequence lengths, while a balanced training recipe maintains both semantic and pixel-level information. Consequently, dNaViT functions as a versatile discrete visual tokenizer, enabling unified encoding across downstream tasks alongside high-fidelity image decoding. Extensive evaluations show that dNaViT matches or exceeds task-specific baselines across 12 visual understanding benchmarks, 4 generation benchmarks, and an image editing benchmark. These findings demonstrate that a compact discrete vocabulary can serve as a universal visual currency and unlock the full potential of fully unified, native multimodal methodology.
  </details>

- **[Direct Trajectory Optimization for Inference-Time Alignment of Text-to-Image Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11821)**  `arXiv:2611.11821`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Huixuan Zhang, Xiaojun Wan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Alignment of text-to-image diffusion models with reward functions is a key challenge in controllable generation. Existing approaches typically rely on optimizing model inputs or fine-tuning denoising networks, which requires computationally expensive backpropagation through large denoising models. In this work, we propose Direct Trajectory Optimization (DTO), a training-free framework that performs alignment by directly optimizing the sampling trajectory of diffusion models. Specifically, DTO treats intermediate denoising scores and sampled noise as the optimization variables, eliminating the need for gradients through the denoising network. To mitigate the out-of-distribution problem, we introduce Reward-Optimized Score Guidance, which interpolates between the optimized scores and the pretrained model predictions to stabilize sampling, while providing a provable theoretical guarantee of reward improvement under constrained distributions. Empirically, DTO improves alignment performance across three diffusion models on two benchmarks, outperforming existing inference-time alignment methods while maintaining high efficiency. These results suggest that trajectory-level optimization is a promising direction for efficient and effective diffusion model alignment.
  </details>

- **[How to "train" a surgeon? Benchmarking generalist agents in surgical scene understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11437)**  `arXiv:2611.11437`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Gengyuan Zhang, Xiao Han, Shijie Zhou, Abdullah Koukash, Jingpei Wu, Jindong Gu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Evaluating general-purpose multimodal large language models (MLLMs) for surgical scene understanding remains limited due to scarce surgical data and fragmented evaluation protocols. We address these challenges through a two-stage evaluation framework for surgical scene understanding. In Stage I, we introduce SurgKnowBench, a diagnostic benchmark that harmonizes public surgical datasets across procedures and tasks and evaluates proprietary and open-source MLLMs to map the boundary of pretrained surgical competence. SurgKnowBench reveals that current models retain partial visual priors but remain weak on relational and procedural understanding and workflow assessment. Building on this failure frontier and motivated by the scaffolded nature of surgical training, we introduce SurgAgentGym, an agentic environment where general-purpose MLLM agents can use bounded knowledge search over SurgWiki and general-purpose visual grounding for surgical tasks. SurgAgentGym evaluates whether generalist agents can improve surgical understanding at inference time through tool-mediated evidence use, without additional surgical training. Our results show that agentic inference narrows the gap mainly for perception-heavy tasks, while fine-grained localization and expert-style assessment remain persistent bottlenecks. This opens a path toward future studies of agentic surgical understanding.
  </details>

- **[MoT3DVG: A Benchmark for Outdoor 3D Visual Grounding with Motion-Aware Descriptions and Temporal Cues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11334)**  `arXiv:2611.11334`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Shijia Zhao, Xusheng Guo, Qiming Xia, Xun Huang, Kezheng Xiong, Zhihong Liu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D visual grounding (3DVG) localizes language-referred objects in 3D scenes, with broad applicability in both indoor and outdoor environments. However, existing 3DVG research focuses on static indoor objects, limiting its use in dynamic outdoor autonomous driving. Motion descriptions, which capture the temporal dynamics of moving objects, offer a promising way to address this challenge. We therefore introduce MoT3DVG, a large-scale dataset for dynamic-aware outdoor 3DVG with temporally evolving motion descriptions. It contains 850 scenarios with 31,128 frames from nuScenes dataset, and provides 144,568 language prompts with motion-aware descriptions for dynamic objects across time. We further propose DynaVG, a novel framework that effectively leverages temporal cues for outdoor 3DVG. In addition to the standard modality-specific encoders, DynaVG introduces a Short-Long Integrated Dynamic Encoding (SLIDE) module before the language-point cloud alignment. SLIDE models both local motion cues and global temporal context via short-term window encoding and long-term window shifting. Extensive experiments demonstrate its state-of-the-art performance on MoT3DVG, validating the effectiveness of motion-aware encoding. Our work reveals open challenges and promising directions for future research in outdoor 3DVG.
  </details>

- **[Viscot-steer: Disentangling and Transferring Visual Chain-of-Thought via Activation Steering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11209)**  `arXiv:2611.11209`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Ningning Wang, Beier Zhu, Xingyu Zhu, Kaiyun Yang, Xun Yang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual chain-of-thought (visual-CoT), also known as "thinking with images", equips LVLMs with the ability to locate and use key visual evidence for reasoning. However, acquiring such visual-CoT capabilities requires costly post-training and tightly couples them to a specific backbone, making transfer to other LVLMs difficult. Existing cross-model transfer methods typically treat visual-CoT as a monolithic capability, using a unified representation that ignores the functional distinction between grounding and reasoning and introduces stage-irrelevant interventions. To address this problem, we propose Viscot-steer, a training-free activation steering framework for transferring visual-CoT capabilities across heterogeneous LVLMs. Specifically, Viscot-steer first disentangles visual-CoT into grounding and reasoning vectors and extracts them from the source-model layers associated with the corresponding capabilities. It then aligns the vectors with the activation space of the target model and steers the grounding and reasoning layers during bounding-box prediction and reasoning-trace generation, respectively. Across benchmarks covering high-resolution visual understanding, general visual perception and reasoning, and mathematical visual reasoning, Viscot-steer transfers capabilities from different visual-CoT models to architecturally diverse LVLMs and outperforms the strongest capability-transfer baseline by an average of 1.86 percentage points on the primary evaluation metrics.
  </details>

- **[Anatomy-Activated Mixture-of-Experts for 3D Medical Vision-Language Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10960)**  `arXiv:2611.10960`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Szymon Płotka, Gizem Mert, Pedro R. A. S. Bassi, Wenxuan Li, Zongwei Zhou, Anna Marcinkiewicz, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Medical Vision-Language Pre-training (VLP) leverages the semantic richness of radiology reports to learn 3D representations, yet current architectures remain fundamentally anatomy-agnostic. While clinical diagnostics rely on organ-specific context, standard encoders apply a uniform, dense parameterization to all volumetric patches, and existing Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) routers gate on isolated local features without explicit access to the organ composition of the scan or crop. We propose Anatomy-Activated Mixture-of-Experts (A$^{2}$MoE), a framework that incorporates anatomical inductive biases within the model's computational path. A$^{2}$MoE introduces two key components: (1) a Histogram Representation Router (HRR) that conditions expert selection on global visual context and a predicted anatomical composition histogram, encouraging anatomy-dependent expert weighting; and (2) an Organ-Query Multi-Scale Projector (OQMSP) that utilizes learnable queries to extract organ-level embeddings for fine-grained alignment without requiring manual annotations at inference. By routing at the patch level rather than the token level, A$^{2}$MoE reduces routing complexity by orders of magnitude while satisfying load balancing by construction. Pre-trained on over 60,000 multi-phase CT scans, A$^{2}$MoE achieves state-of-the-art performance across segmentation, classification, and detection benchmarks. Furthermore, we demonstrate that our anatomically grounded inductive biases support robust generalization to MRI and ultrasound, establishing a scalable foundation for medical representation learning. The code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Fine-resolution land-use segmentation and synthetic image-label augmentation in the Kathmandu Valley](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10847)**  `arXiv:2611.10847`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Pranil Parajuli, Sugam Dahal, Kiran Chandra Dahal_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Parcel-scale land-use data is missing for most rapidly urbanizing Global South cities, as current segmentation architectures rely on multispectral imagery and planned layouts. We introduce a hand-annotated six-class land-use corpus covering 202 km$^2$ of Kathmandu Valley across 408 fine-resolution RGB scenes. The dataset is uniquely challenging: RGB spectral similarity is high, class imbalance approaches 100:1, and key structural features like rivers measure just two to three pixels wide at a standard hierarchical decoder's prediction stride. Evaluating five architectures reveals that standard random data splitting conceals massive spatial leakage. Switching to a strict sequence-block split drops performance by up to 0.095 mIoU. This penalty exceeds the performance gap between the architectures themselves. We modified the SegFormer baseline by integrating a half-resolution residual detail path, boundary-reliability reweighting, a topology-aware loss, and a presence-balanced sampler. Adding only 1.9% more parameters, the enhanced architecture improves mean IoU across every test fold, with gains concentrated in rare, thin-structure classes. However, both models achieve identical results on the external LoveDA benchmark. Finally, augmenting with diffusion-generated pairs at matched compute degraded accuracy across all architectures, especially harming rare classes it aimed to improve. This demonstrates that blindly incorporating synthetic data can severely damage performance, highlighting a critical need to rigorously evaluate and filter generated samples before training.
  </details>

- **[WorldFoundry: Towards Unified Infrastructure for World Intelligence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10771)**  `arXiv:2611.10771`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Juanxi Tian, Jiadong Hou, Baiang Li, Xin Lin, Zhengbo Jiao, Weijia Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual generative models increasingly blur the line between synthesizing observations and simulating environments. Yet visual plausibility alone does not constitute successful world modeling: compelling outputs may lack persistent state, physical dynamics, or causally consistent responses to interactions. We define a generative world model as a predictive dynamical system that maintains a persistent, history-conditioned representation of an environment, updates that representation in response to actions or interventions, and generates temporally coherent trajectories that reflect outcomes under observed and counterfactual interventions. From this definition, we derive an intelligence-oriented framework spanning perceptual grounding, persistent state modeling, controllable transformation, dynamics-aware prediction, and closed-loop interaction. We present WorldFoundry, open-source infrastructure for reproducible inference and evaluation across video, 3D and 4D, embodied action, and interactive world systems. WorldFoundry unifies model and benchmark discovery, runtime and asset preparation, execution orchestration, artifact management, and interactive visualization, while modality-specific adapters preserve native inputs, execution semantics, output representations, and evaluation protocols. Explicit manifests, durable artifact contracts, and evidence-aware scorecards separate execution readiness from evaluative claims, making each claim traceable to its supporting artifacts. The catalog contains 270 model entries, 74 benchmarks, and 62 registered metric entries, with readiness and execution evidence recorded separately. WorldFoundry enables reproducible comparison of persistent, controllable dynamics across heterogeneous systems, advancing the field from perceptual synthesis toward measurable world intelligence.
  </details>

- **[Tiny Evidence Region Localization with Selective Contrastive On-Policy Self-Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10734)**  `arXiv:2611.10734`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Yujuan Pang, Wenhao Xu, Yinglei Teng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models have made progress in general visual tasks. However, limitations remain in fine-grained visual tasks requiring evidence from minute image regions. Existing methods primarily train models using evidence-related regions while overlooking the influence of extensive background regions, resulting in a limited ability to resist interference from irrelevant regions. We propose selective positive-negative on-policy self-distillation (SPN-OPSD), which considers both evidence and background regions. Supervision from both views improves the model's ability to resist interference from irrelevant regions. Furthermore, selective token-level distillation provides more informative supervision, improving the model's ability to attend to evidence regions in Fine-Grained Visual Tasks. On Qwen3.5-2B, SPN-OPSD improves accuracy on V* Bench, ZoomBench, and MME-RealWorld-Lite from 76.44%, 45.92%, and 57.43% to 84.82%, 54.56%, and 59.56%, respectively. Similar gains are also observed on Qwen3.5-0.8B.
  </details>

- **[EndoSCOP-V: A Multi-Turn Video Understanding Evaluation Framework for Multimodal Models in Endoscopy Reporting and Clinical Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10626)**  `arXiv:2611.10626`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Abhishek Tyagi, Tanmay Jain, Vilas Biradar, Krithi Krishna Koduri, Misba Hul Bashir Masoodi, Swetha Rapolu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Endoscopic diagnosis unfolds over native video, where a single positive finding during traversal of the gastrointestinal tract sets off a multi-minute chain of reasoning over location, morphology, severity and intervention. Existing endoscopy multimodal benchmarks test only static images or single-disease videos, which miss the temporal and clinical reasoning that real-world reporting demands. We introduce EndoSCOP-V, a video-grounded multi-turn evaluation framework for structured endoscopy reporting and clinical reasoning. It contains 1,307 real-world endoscopy and colonoscopy videos (63.2 h; 97% 1080p) averaging about 174 s. Its 20,274 question-answer turns are instantiated deterministically from clinician-filled structured endoscopy-reporting EHRs, so no per-case annotation is synthetic or LLM-rephrased. They come from 1,033 clinician-reviewed templates, about 18 per disease, that span a clinically aligned 9-task x 30-sub-task reporting taxonomy over 54 World Endoscopy Organization (WEO) MST 3.0 diseases. 22% of procedures carry co-existing pathologies, up to six per case. A Markovian context-pruned oracle-forcing protocol evaluates the dialogue turn by turn, injecting the gold answer before each subsequent turn so that per-turn reasoning is isolated from cascading dialogue errors. A panel of 15 board-certified endoscopists sets a 78% strict-accuracy baseline on 1,500 questions. Across 14 open-weight LMMs, the best 27B-class model reaches 59.8% strict accuracy, falling to 53.4% once format-trivial disease-rejection items are excluded. On a between-subjects spot-check, the best of 3 frontier proprietary models trails the clinician panel by 23 pp on strict NDR accuracy. Sub-task analysis reveals consistent gaps in quantitative measurement, multi-variable grading and intervention reasoning on long-horizon video. Data: Hugging Face (EndoSCOP-V). Code: GitHub (abhi25t/EndoSCOP-V).
  </details>

- **[Reason First, Retrieve What Matters: Hypothesis-Guided Keyframe Selection for Long Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10609)**  `arXiv:2611.10609`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Yisong Hu, Junbo Wang, Yuke Li, Jiangbin Zheng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Long video understanding requires models to identify and reason over sparse, temporally distributed evidence within limited frame budgets. Existing keyframe selection methods typically retrieve query-relevant frames from a densely sampled candidate set before inference. However, global image-text matching incurs unnecessary computation when a small set of uniformly sampled frames already provides sufficient evidence. Query relevance alone may also overlook evidence that distinguishes competing answers, while one-shot selection offers no opportunity to correct earlier selection errors. We propose HypoKFS, a training-free, plug-and-play framework that couples keyframe selection with iterative inference by multimodal large language models (MLLMs). HypoKFS first reasons over sparsely sampled frames to construct an initial hypothesis space. The resulting answer distribution determines whether additional frame selection is needed. When needed, the retained hypotheses guide the selection of discriminative frames, followed by renewed inference and hypothesis refinement. This iterative process directs additional visual computation toward unresolved hypotheses while avoiding unnecessary global frame matching. Experiments on Video-MME, LongVideoBench, and MLVU demonstrate consistent improvements across multiple MLLM backbones. HypoKFS improves accuracy by up to 6.7 percentage points and achieves 72.9% accuracy on Video-MME at only 1.3% of the reference frame-hypothesis matching cost.
  </details>

- **[MotionGraphicsBench: Evaluating Motion Graphics Generation with Programmatic Verification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10592)**  `arXiv:2611.10592`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Jiaju Ma, Ashvini Kumar Jindal, Chuhua Wang, Sumithra Bhakthavatsalam_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large language models can generate motion graphics from static images and natural language requests, but it remains difficult to evaluate how well models can follow requests that combine spatial, appearance, and temporal requirements. We introduce MotionGraphicsBench, an expert-curated benchmark of 100 instances for animating an input static SVG with a motion edit prompt. By supplying all required visual elements, our editing task reflects a designer-like workflow and isolates motion generation from graphic generation. Derived from real-world Lottie motion designs, our benchmark contains diverse combinations of spatio-temporal animation behaviors. Each benchmark instance pairs its model input with a deterministic, human-readable verification program that accepts different valid implementations and reports which prompt requirements pass or fail, rather than comparing against a reference animation or using a VLM judge. We construct these programs with a multi-agent pipeline using human-labeled animations and synthesized test cases. On a balanced test set of 180 animations, our programs achieve 93.3% raw agreement with human judgments, compared with 83.3% for a GPT-6 Astra VLM baseline, and outperform the baseline in both approval and rejection recall. Across 47 configurations from 32 LLMs and 12 providers, pass@1 ranges from 6.0% to 67.7%. Across 15 models evaluated at their highest available reasoning effort with 32 attempts per instance, additional attempts improve every model, but pass@32 still ranges from 38.0% to 95.0%. Our analysis of verification programs reveals that generated animations often fail to match reference poses, follow requested motion trajectories, or coordinate related events. We release our benchmark atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Mental Imagery That Matters: Curing Latent Collapse in Multimodal Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10423)**  `arXiv:2611.10423`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Mi Luo, Rohan Dugad, Zihui Xue, Alex Dimakis, Kristen Grauman_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Mental simulation, the ability to reason by constructing and updating internal visual states, is central to flexible human cognition, but remains elusive in current multimodal large language models. Latent multimodal reasoning offers a promising route toward this capability by interleaving text with continuous visual latent blocks, allowing models to maintain internal visual workspaces without decoding them into pixels. In this paper, we ask whether these latent visual states actually matter. Through a direct perturbation analysis across representative latent-reasoning MLLMs, we find that they often do not: dropping, replacing, or shuffling generated visual latents has only minor effects on the model's own final-answer probability, while equivalent perturbations to the linguistic reasoning trace are substantially more damaging. We call this failure mode Mental Simulation Collapse (MSC): the model appears to perform interleaved visual reasoning, but routes most answer-relevant computation through text. We further diagnose that MSC follows naturally from existing training objectives: visual alignment can make generated latents resemble helper-image embeddings, but it does not require the decoder to rely on them; subsequent text-only supervision further leaves the semantic role of the latent pathway unidentifiable. To address this, we introduce AnchorSim (Anchored Mental Simulation), a contrastive distillation framework that trains models to make their generated visual latents consequential. Across multiple visual reasoning benchmarks, AnchorSim improves performance, while diagnostic re-evaluation shows that visual perturbations now meaningfully affect predictions. Our results expose a fundamental failure mode of latent multimodal reasoning and demonstrate that mental simulation must be anchored to model behavior in order to matter.
  </details>

- **[Frequency-Structure Complementary Modeling for Hyperspectral Image Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10413)**  `arXiv:2611.10413`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Song Qiya, Tian Siyu, Li Shutao, Dian Renwei, Sun Long, Liu Jinyang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing methods mainly rely on convolutional aggregation or attention-based spatial-spectral interaction. However, conventional feature-domain attention does not explicitly characterize variation patterns along spatial-token and learned latent-channel coordinates, while spatially shared local operators lack the flexibility to adapt neighborhood aggregation to heterogeneous structures. To address these limitations, we propose a Frequency-Structure Complementary Modeling Network (FSCM-Net), which couples frequency-aware dependency learning with structure-adaptive local refinement. Specifically, a Spatial-Spectral Frequency Calibration Attention (SSFCA) module constructs spatial-token affinities from Fourier-interacted query-key representations and builds latent-channel affinities after input-conditioned frequency filtering along learned channel coordinates. The latent-channel response is then calibrated by a learned shared gain and combined with the spatial-token response. Complementarily, a Structure-Coherent Dynamic Feature Fusion (SC-DFF) module combines regularized structure-tensor cues from MSI features with context-modulated HSI detail representations to generate channel- and position-dependent aggregation kernels. This design makes local HSI refinement responsive to smooth regions, edges, and textures while maintaining spectral fidelity. The two modules are progressively integrated within a multiscale encoder-decoder to coordinate representation-level interaction and structure-adaptive reconstruction. Experiments on two simulated benchmarks and one real remote-sensing dataset demonstrate favorable performance against representative fusion methods in spatial detail reconstruction and spectral preservation.
  </details>

- **[PoseBench: Probing Pose Awareness in Visual Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10347)**  `arXiv:2611.10347`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Most Nilufa Yeasmin, Draix Wyatt, Ruyi Lian_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pose awareness characterizes the extent to which a learned feature space aligns with the underlying object-pose manifold, such that observations with similar poses exhibit greater feature similarity than those with increasingly different poses. While such structure is desirable for pose-sensitive 3D vision tasks, visual foundation models pre-trained on individual 2D images typically lack explicit 3D or pose supervision. This raises a fundamental question: Do 2D visual foundation models exhibit pose awareness after large-scale pretraining? To answer this question, we introduce PoseBench, a systematic benchmark to probe pose awareness in pretrained visual features. Our evaluation follows a progressive design: held-out rendered views probe intrinsic pose-aware structure under controlled conditions, while real-image queries evaluate how robustly this structure remains accessible under real-world variations. We further introduce pose-graph-based refinement as a complementary probe of compatibility between feature neighborhoods and the underlying pose manifold. Across 31 publicly available checkpoints from 10 pretraining families, PoseBench reveals that pose awareness is an implicit but non-uniform property of 2D visual foundation models, and consistent gains from pose-graph refinement suggest that feature spaces retain structure compatible with the underlying pose manifold. PoseBench therefore provides a systematic basis for understanding and selecting pretrained features for pose-sensitive 3D vision tasks.
  </details>

- **[LoCo: Selective Local Competition for Discriminative Open-Vocabulary Multi-Label Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10321)**  `arXiv:2611.10321`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Zhuoming Li, Bo Han, Xiaoyu Wang, Yuheng Jia_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Open-Vocabulary Multi-Label Recognition (OV-MLR) aims to identify all queried semantic concepts present in an image. Existing methods mainly improve global image-text matching or text-side representations, leaving local prediction largely under-explored. In this paper, we revisit local prediction and reveal that it can serve as a strong discriminative branch once two properties are properly handled: spatial consistency of local visual features and compatibility-aware local competition. This perspective is motivated by local semantic sparsity: each image region is related to only a small subset of queried concepts, while compatible concepts of different granularities or types may still coexist locally. We instantiate this perspective with LoCo, a selective Local Competition framework for OV-MLR. LoCo adopts a spatially consistent visual encoder to reduce local feature entanglement, and introduces Selective Softmax to impose competition only among locally incompatible categories while preserving compatible responses. Multi-scale inference further improves local prediction at different scales. LoCo can also be ensembled with an existing global predictor to provide complementary image-level context. Experiments on four multi-label benchmarks show that standalone LoCo improves average F1-score and mAP over TagCLIP by 3.4% and 3.8%, respectively. LoCo + DualPrompt achieves the highest averages among the compared methods, with gains of 4.8% and 5.2% over TagCLIP. Our results suggest that local semantic sparsity offers a useful basis for developing more discriminative open-vocabulary multi-label recognition methods. The code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[CXR-CMFA: A Cross-Modal Feature Aggregation Network for Chest X-Ray Pathology Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10174)**  `arXiv:2611.10174`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Talukder Hasnat Zadid, Jahin Sultana, Felipe Bochini Brisola, Kishore Roy, Mysha Rahman, Syeda Asrafa Islam, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Chest X-ray (CXR) interpretation remains challenging because subtle pathology can be obscured by overlapping anatomy, image-quality variation, and limited visual evidence in isolated radiographs. Although image-only deep learning models provide useful baselines, they discard the complementary diagnostic context available in radiology reports, while large vision-language models can be computationally expensive for practical deployment. This paper presents CXR-CMFA, a lightweight cross-modal feature aggregation network for binary CXR pathology classification. The proposed framework combines a dual-stream visual encoder, using EfficientNet-B0 for global anatomical representation and DenseNet-121 for local pathological detail, with a TF-IDF-MLP report encoder. Two fusion modules integrate these modalities: the Dual-Modal Spatial Attention Module (DMSAM) uses the report embedding to gate global and local visual features, and the Cross-Modal Gated Feature Fusion (CGFF) module learns per-instance weights for visual and textual contributions. Experiments on the MIMIC-CXR benchmark, using the provided train, validation, and test splits, show that CXR-CMFA achieves 93.45% accuracy, 99.88% precision, 91.8% recall, 95.7% F1-score, and 99.19% AUC on the held-out test set. The model produces only four false positives among 984 normal test cases, supporting its suitability for high-specificity screening workflows. An ablation study further demonstrates a 22.47 percentage-point AUC improvement over the global-image-only variant, confirming the value of adaptive cross-modal fusion. These results indicate that compact report-conditioned fusion can provide strong discriminative performance without relying on large pretrained language models.
  </details>

- **[A Privacy-Aware Deep Learning Benchmark for Facial Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10097)**  `arXiv:2611.10097`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Larissa Palhares, Maria Machado, Hudson Bruno, Raphael Ramos, Andrew Vale, Barbara Barbosa, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Facial Expression Recognition (FER) traditionally relies on raw RGB images, introducing privacy risks, sensitivity to illumination variations, and susceptibility to identity-confounding visual shortcuts. As an alternative, this work proposes a lightweight, privacy-aware pipeline using exclusively geometric representations-facial landmarks (478 points) and blendshapes (52 coefficients, ARKit standard)-eliminating persistent raw video storage. Six neural architectures trained from scratch on fused CREMA-D and RAVDESS datasets were benchmarked against an ImageNet-pretrained ResNet50 baseline under a 5-fold subject-independent cross-validation protocol. Geometric models consistently outperformed the pixel baseline on unseen subjects with 12-124 times fewer parameters, while demonstrating superior robustness on subtle emotional expressions such as sadness. Ablation studies reveal that blendshapes capture the primary discriminative signal, while landmarks provide essential complementary spatial topology. These results demonstrate that compact geometric representations preserve affective semantics, eliminate downstream transfer learning dependency on visual backbones, and enable efficient, privacy-aware emotion recognition.
  </details>

- **[EarthJev: Efficient Visual Evidence Acquisition and Decision for UHR Remote Sensing Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10005)**  `arXiv:2611.10005`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Pengyu Dai, Yao Zhang, Jian Song, Junjue Wang, Yafei Ou, Yuzeng Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Ultra-high-resolution (UHR) remote sensing visual question answering (VQA) requires locating fine-grained evidence in large scenes under limited visual input budgets. Active observation addresses this challenge through selective exploration, but repeated generative judgments can make region search costly. Its decisions also concern distinct prediction targets: spatial pointer validity, evidence presence, and reasoning correctness. We propose EarthJev, a framework that separates region scoring from answer generation and uses target-specific decision signals while keeping the answering model frozen. A spatial pointer verifier uses visual and geometric cues to guide exploration or bypass search. An evidence-aware decision mechanism scores compact candidate views in batches using a lightweight head on frozen representations, without generating textual region judgments. Format-specific confidence calibration then guides selection between candidate answers generated from memory and overview presentations of the acquired evidence. Across five benchmarks, EarthJev achieves a mean accuracy of 53.06% with Qwen3.5-9B, outperforming ZoomSearch with the same answering model by 2.33 to 14.73 percentage points per benchmark while reducing mean latency from 11.30 - 1.67 seconds per question. With four additional frozen answering models, model-specific fitting of the decision modules and calibration mappings yields mean accuracy gains of 3.41 - 6.53 percentage points over the corresponding single-pass baselines. Code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[HALMES: Knowing When to Intervene in LVLM Hallucination Mitigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09957)**  `arXiv:2611.09957`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Ruining Hu, Jiaqi Lu, Xiao Liu, Ying Shen, Yu Wang, Lin Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) have demonstrated strong capabilities in multimodal tasks. Nevertheless, their outputs remain prone to object hallucinations that are inconsistent with visual content. Contrastive decoding, as a training-free inference-time mitigation approach, typically intervenes in the output distribution at every decoding step. However, such indiscriminate full-stage intervention may disrupt originally correct generations and even induce new hallucinations. To address this issue, we first analyze the relationship between internal attention features and hallucinated token segments in LVLMs, revealing that hallucination-related abnormalities already emerge progressively in preceding token segments. Based on this observation, we propose HALMES, a lightweight plug-and-play module that identifies hallucination precursor segments from attention features during generation and selectively triggers contrastive decoding. HALMES can be integrated with various full-stage contrastive decoding methods, transforming indiscriminate intervention into on-demand targeted correction. Extensive experiments across different LVLM architectures and multiple benchmarks show that HALMES further improves the hallucination mitigation performance of existing contrastive decoding methods while reducing the negative impact of full-stage intervention on normal outputs, validating the importance of modeling and selectively intervening on hallucination-prone segments.
  </details>

- **[Neural Image Compression with Internal-External Prior Fusion and Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.07644)**  `arXiv:2611.07644`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Tiancheng Si, Wenlong Xu, Dahua Gao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing context models for learned image com- pression rely primarily on internal priors from the current image, overlooking common patterns and typical textures shared across natural images. To address this limitation, we propose an Internal-External Prior Fusion-Alignment (IE-PFA) context model built upon quadtree spatial-channel joint modeling, where a learnable dictionary shared by the encoder and decoder serves as an external prior. Specifically, an RWKV-based Spatial- Channel Prior Fusion (RSCPF) module efficiently fuses inter- nal and external priors along spatial and channel dimensions with linear complexity, while a training-only Self-Distilled Prior Alignment (SDPA) mechanism aligns them without inference overhead. Moreover, our model supports variable-rate coding over 64 quality levels within a single network and is accelerated by custom CUDA operators. Experiments on seven benchmarks demonstrate state-of-the-art rate-distortion performance, achiev- ing an average BD-Rate saving of 21.65% over VTM-22.0-Intra, with only 29.9M parameters and the fastest coding speed among advanced neural codecs (61 ms encoding and 52 ms decoding). Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[FOCUS: Benchmarking Retinal Model Generalization from Foundation Vision Encoders to Multimodal LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33158)**  `arXiv:2609.33158`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _David Restrepo, Chenwei Wu, Luis Filipe Nakayama, Miguel L. Martins, Stergios Christodoulidis, Maria Vakalopoulou, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Progress in AI-based retinal image analysis has accelerated with the adoption of foundation models, yet evaluating their real-world reliability remains challenging. Performance reported on a single dataset does not capture how models behave under dataset shift, across clinical definitions, or for different patient subgroups. This limitation is particularly critical in medical imaging, where robustness, calibration, and fairness are essential for safe deployment. We introduce FOCUS (Foundation Ophthalmic Cross-Dataset Understanding under Shift), a cross-dataset benchmark evaluating retinal fundus models across vision-only encoder models (VM), vision-language dual-encoder models (VLM), and multimodal large language models (MLLM). FOCUS harmonizes binary diabetic retinopathy, referable diabetic retinopathy, and glaucomatous optic neuropathy tasks across ten public datasets spanning diverse geographies, acquisition conditions, and label protocols. The benchmark evaluates models through a unified analysis layer that measures ranking performance, calibration, subgroup disparities, and image-quality robustness. We present a large-scale evaluation covering 532 base configurations and 228 MLLM configurations adapted via LoRA fine-tuning. Results show no model family consistently dominates across tasks and datasets: general VM encoders achieve the strongest average ranking performance, medical MLLMs are competitive but variable, and dual-encoder VLMs benefit substantially from lightweight adaptation. Fine-tuning improves in-domain performance but exhibits heterogeneous transfer to external datasets, particularly in calibration. FOCUS provides a practical framework and public benchmark to assess generalization, reliability, and robustness beyond single-dataset leaderboards.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Uniform Restoration: Empowering All-in-One Restoration with Pixel-Level Multimodal Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09482)**  `arXiv:2608.09482`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Chunxiao Liu, Wei Liu, Anbin Xiong, Erli Meng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  All-in-one image restoration is a unified low-level vision task that aims to effectively recover high-quality images from inputs degraded by various types and levels of corruption using a single model. Recent works have achieved remarkable progress by learning degradation-adaptive prompts or network architectures. However, these methods typically apply a uniform restoration strategy across the entire image, neglecting the fact that different regions may suffer from distinct degradation types and varying degrees of severity. In contrast, we propose to perform restoration at the pixel level, thereby enabling more fine-grained and precise control over the restoration process. Specifically, we present MGN-AIR, a novel pixel-level restoration framework for all-in-one image restoration. Our approach first learns to estimate a pixel-level visual prompt. Then, it leverages both textual and visual prompts to provide global and local degradation cues, guiding the model on where to look and how to restore at each pixel. We conduct extensive experiments on multiple all-in-one image restoration benchmarks, covering a wide range of tasks including denoising, deraining, deblurring, dehazing, desnowing, and low-light enhancement. Experimental results demonstrate that our proposed method consistently and significantly outperforms existing approaches.
  </details>

- **[Spatial-IQ: Deconstructing Spatial Intelligence via Hierarchical Capability Tests](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.22864)**  `arXiv:2607.22864`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Patrick Rim, Tom Long, Ekta Prashnani, Ruth Rosenholtz, Ben Boudaoud, Peter Xenopoulos, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) excel at visual interpretation but fail on spatial reasoning tasks that humans solve reliably. Existing benchmarks evaluate these models as black boxes, limiting their ability to identify the underlying causes of lower performance: when a model fails a spatial reasoning task, it remains difficult to ascertain whether the hurdle is perceptual, such as recognizing object boundaries, or cognitive, such as reasoning about occlusion to infer hidden geometry. We introduce Spatial-IQ, a hierarchical diagnostic framework that decomposes object counting in stacked 3D structures into 9 perceptual and cognitive sub-tasks organized by the developmental stages of human spatial cognition, with mental rotation as an additional target probe. Using NVIDIA Isaac Sim, we procedurally generated a diverse dataset of roughly 80,000 stacked 3D structures with per-task ground truth. We evaluate models across three output formats (free-response text, multiple-choice images, and image editing) alongside a human baseline. The Spatial-IQ framework shows that top-performing models often succeed at the target task (object counting) without succeeding on the lower-level sub-tasks intended to support it, and that models differ in how much of these hierarchical chains they preserve, often revealing shortcut behavior that raw target-task accuracy alone would obscure. Finally, we demonstrate that training models with chain-of-thought (CoT) supervision over our hierarchical sub-tasks, combined with reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards, significantly improves both spatial consistency across sub-tasks and target-task accuracy, supporting the value of the proposed decomposition as both a diagnostic tool and a training signal.
  </details>

- **[Beyond RGB: Benchmarking and Enhancing MLLMs for Hyperspectral Image Understanding via Training-Free Reasoning Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08884)**  `arXiv:2604.08884`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Xinyu Zhang, Zurong Mai, Qingmei Li, Xiaoya Fan, Zjin Liao, Haoyuan Liang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have achieved strong performance on RGB image understanding, yet their ability to use spectral evidence beyond the visible range remains largely unexplored. Hyperspectral imagery (HSI) provides dense spectral measurements that reveal material and environmental cues unavailable in RGB, but current MLLMs cannot directly ingest high-dimensional HSI. To study this gap, we introduce HM-Bench, an evidence-grounded benchmark for hyperspectral image understanding with MLLMs. HM-Bench contains 19,337 question--answer pairs from 2,178 hyperspectral samples across 13 task categories, covering general perception, spectral reasoning, and spatial--spectral reasoning. To make HSI accessible to the native image--text interfaces of existing MLLMs, we further propose VSR$^{2}$, a training-free Visual--Spectral--Report Reasoning framework. VSR$^{2}$ represents each HSI sample with three aligned views: an RGB image for visual semantics and spatial context, a PCA-based image for dominant spectral variation, and a structured report for quantitative spectral--spatial evidence. Under a controlled RGB-only versus VSR$^{2}$ evaluation protocol, experiments on 17 representative MLLMs show that HSI information improves average accuracy from 38.90% to 40.95%, with gains varying substantially across models and task categories. These results indicate that HSI information (beyond RGB) is useful for current MLLMs, while robust hyperspectral reasoning remains an open challenge.
  </details>

- **[Vision Transformers for Zero-Shot Clustering of Animal Images: A Comparative Benchmarking Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.03894)**  `arXiv:2602.03894`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Hugo Markoff, Stefan Hein Bengtson, Michael Ørsted_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Manual labelling of animal images remains a significant bottleneck in ecological research, limiting the scale and efficiency of biodiversity monitoring efforts. This study investigates whether state-of-the-art Vision Transformer (ViT) foundation models can reduce thousands of unlabelled animal images directly to species-level clusters. We present a comprehensive benchmarking framework evaluating five ViT models combined with five dimensionality reduction techniques and four clustering algorithms, two supervised and two unsupervised, across 60 species (30 mammals and 30 birds), with each test using a random subset of 200 validated images per species. We investigate when clustering succeeds at species-level, where it fails, and whether clustering within the species-level reveals ecologically meaningful patterns such as sex, age, or phenotypic variation. Our primary, fully unsupervised finding is that DINOv3 embeddings with t-SNE and HDBSCAN reach a species-level V-measure of 0.943 with no prior knowledge of the number of species, rejecting only 1.14% of images as outliers requiring expert review. Assigning one label per cluster reduces manual labelling actions by 98.3%, with 94.6% of clustered images labelled correctly, close to the accuracy of manual annotation. When the true number of species is supplied, supervised hierarchical clustering raises this to 0.958, which we report as an upper bound as the species count is typically unknown in real deployments. We further demonstrate robustness to realistic long-tailed distributions of species and show that intentional over-clustering can reveal intra-specific variation including age classes, sexual dimorphism, and pelage differences. We introduce an open-source benchmarking toolkit and provide recommendations for ecologists to select appropriate methods for sorting their specific taxonomic groups and data.
  </details>

- **[SANEval: Open-Vocabulary Compositional Benchmarks with Failure-mode Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.00249)**  `arXiv:2602.00249`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Rishav Pramanik, Ian E. Nielsen, Jeff Smith, Saurav Pandit, Ravi P. Ramachandran, Zhaozheng Yin_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-image (T2I) models struggle with compositional prompts requiring multiple objects, bound attributes, accurate counts, or spatial relations. Existing benchmarks face limits from fixed detector vocabularies, aggregated scores, and varying human-validation protocols. We introduce SANEval (Spatial, Attribute, and Numeracy Evaluation), an open-vocabulary compositional benchmark with two key contributions: (i) a public dataset of ~5,250 prompts paired with images from six state-of-the-art T2I models, split into algorithmically generated Simple and fully human-written Hard partitions across five compositional tasks, and (ii) a modular evaluation pipeline combining an LLM prompt parser, an open-vocabulary detector with LLM synonym expansion, and scorers for attribute binding, spatial relations, and numeracy. Each scorer emits numerical scores and diagnostic feedback for missing, extra, or mis-bound elements. Validated against human ratings (n = 500), SANEval achieves positive Pearson correlations across all categories (overall r = 0.222), outperforming CompBench++ (-0.016) and GenEval (-0.030). A manual audit of 24,012 detections shows 96.58% precision, with ablation confirming that synonym expansion is critical to pipeline performance. Dataset: [this https URL](this https URL). Pipeline: [this https URL](this https URL).
  </details>

- **[Towards Continual Expansion of Data Coverage: Automatic Text-guided Edge-case Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.26158)**  `arXiv:2509.26158`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Kyeongryeol Go_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The performance of deep neural networks is strongly influenced by the quality of their training data. However, mitigating dataset bias by manually curating challenging edge cases remains a major bottleneck. To address this, we propose an automated pipeline for text-guided edge-case synthesis. Our approach employs a Large Language Model, fine-tuned via preference learning, to rephrase image captions into diverse textual prompts that steer a Text-to-Image model toward generating difficult visual scenarios. Evaluated on the FishEye8K object detection benchmark, our method achieves superior robustness, surpassing both naive augmentation and manually engineered prompts. This work establishes a scalable framework that shifts data curation from manual effort to automated, targeted synthesis, offering a promising direction for developing more reliable and continuously improving AI systems. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Speculative Decoding Reimagined for Multimodal Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.14260)**  `arXiv:2505.14260`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Luxi Lin, Zhihang Lin, Zhanpeng Zeng, Rongrong Ji_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) deliver strong performance across vision-language tasks but suffer from high autoregressive decoding latency. Although speculative decoding can accelerate Large Language Models (LLMs) without changing their output distribution, directly applying existing approaches to MLLMs yields limited speedups because text and visual tokens exhibit distinct characteristics. We present Multimodal Speculative Decoding (MSD), a framework specifically designed for efficient MLLM inference. MSD is motivated by two key observations: text and visual tokens should be handled separately during drafting, and an effective draft model requires both strong language modeling and visual perception capabilities. Accordingly, MSD decouples text and visual token processing in the draft model and applies token-specific strategies to improve drafting efficiency. We further introduce a two-stage training strategy. The draft model is first trained on text-only instruction-tuning data to strengthen its language modeling ability and is then progressively exposed to multimodal data to develop visual perception while preserving its language competence. Extensive experiments across multimodal benchmarks demonstrate that MSD achieves up to $2.29\times$ inference speedup for LLaVA-1.5-7B and $2.46\times$ for LLaVA-1.5-13B without sacrificing generation quality. These results establish MSD as an effective and general approach for accelerating multimodal autoregressive generation. Our code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[Reframe: Observation-Aligned Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation for Agentic Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12886)**  `arXiv:2611.12886`  `cs.CV`  
  _Haozhi Yuan, Handong Li, Yifan Zhang, Qianshan Wei_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
