Building on our prior Segment-then-Splat framework, we present RefineSplat, a reconstruction-guided extension that retains its object-centric paradigm: it first tracks objects in 2D and then reconstructs each object with a dedicated set of 3D Gaussians, preserving explicit object-Gaussian correspondence for unified open-vocabulary segmentation and retrieval. To improve robustness, we further close the loop between 2D tracking and 3D reconstruction. Since off-the-shelf video trackers can produce unreliable masks that propagate errors into object reconstruction, we introduce a reconstruction-guided feedback mechanism that refines object tracking using rendered masks and multi-view consistency. Moreover, instead of assigning each object a single language embedding, we propose a viewpoint-aware embedding assignment strategy, where each object maintains multiple view-clustered embeddings to capture view-dependent semantic cues. We also introduce Blender-OVS, a synthetic benchmark of static and dynamic sequences with exact object meshes and point clouds, enabling direct evaluation of extracted geometry. Experiments on static and dynamic benchmarks show improved rendered-view segmentation and lower object-level Chamfer distances, with competitive scene reconstruction quality.
  </details>

- **[DAPointRWKV: Domain Adaptive Vision RWKV for Point Cloud Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10353)**  `arXiv:2611.10353`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yinghui Li, Qianyu Zhou, Hao Yang, Richard Dazeley, Ye Zhu, Xuequan Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Domain adaptive point cloud completion (DA PCC) aims to bridge the geometric discrepancy between labeled source domains and unlabeled target domains for accurate 3D shape completion. Existing methods based on CNNs, Transformers, and Mamba-like sequence models still face challenges from limited receptive fields, high computational complexity, insufficient geometric bias, and serialization sensitivity. Recently, Receptance Weighted Key Value (RWKV) provides a linear-complexity alternative for long-range sequence modeling. However, adapting RWKV to DA PCC remains challenging due to the lack of explicit geometric priors, the mismatch between recurrent modeling and coarse-to-fine generation, and unstable target-domain adaptation. To address these challenges, we propose DAPointRWKV, a novel RWKV-based framework for DA PCC. DAPointRWKV consists of three components: Geometry-Aware RWKV (GA-RWKV) Backbone, RWKV-Oriented Progressive Decoder (RWKV-PD), and Dual Structural Regularization (DSR). Specifically, GA-RWKV introduces geometry-aware recurrent representation learning, RWKV-PD bridges RWKV token representations with coarse-to-fine reconstruction through local token-guided refinement, and DSR stabilizes target-domain transfer through visible-structure anchoring and complete-shape hypothesis stabilization. Extensive experiments on synthetic and real-world benchmarks demonstrate that DAPointRWKV consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods.
  </details>

- **[MaRiO: Multi-agent Collaborative Reasoning via Shared Observations in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10344)**  `arXiv:2611.10344`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nathaniel Redmond, Fidel Omar Tito Cruz, Devansh Sharma, Shehreen Azad, Sirshapan Mitra, Shruti Vyas, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) show strong visual perception capabilities, but existing evaluations largely focus on single-view or multi-view settings with shared camera parameters, leaving the challenge of multi-agent collaborative scenarios underexplored. In such settings, multiple agents observe a shared environment from independent viewpoints, and reliable decisions require reasoning across these independent observations of the same environment. To study this problem, we introduce MaRiO, a benchmark for evaluating multi-agent collaborative reasoning, and evaluate 23 state-of-the-art MLLMs on it. Our analysis reveals three key findings: (1) tasks governed by static perception are generally easier than tasks that require transformational event sequences; (2) most models struggle to resolve cross-agent correspondences, with errors increasing with scene complexity; and (3) models show limited ability to leverage geometric cues present in the scene. As an initial step toward addressing these challenges, we explore geometry-guided synthetic view augmentation that generates intermediate transition frames between agent observations, providing auxiliary spatial context that improves performance in certain categories. Overall, our results highlight that multi-agent collaborative reasoning remains an open challenge and an important direction for multimodal embodied systems. The code is available on the project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[TimeTraveler: Temporal Strategy Planning with Time Dictionary for Streaming Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10337)**  `arXiv:2611.10337`  `cs.CV`  
  _Minkuk Kim, Junho Kim, Seong Tae Kim_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Streaming video understanding (SVU) requires models to answer user queries over continuously evolving video streams. Unlike offline video QA, SVU must handle queries whose evidence may lie in the past, appear in the current scene, or emerge only after future observations. Existing streaming video systems often rely on compact visual memories or fixed evidence-access schemes, which can lose fine-grained details and fail to adapt the answering process to the temporal intent of each query. In this paper, we propose TimeTraveler, a streaming VQA framework that performs temporal strategy planning with a Time Dictionary. Inspired by the need to search across different temporal regions of a stream, TimeTraveler stores each observed moment as a timestamp-indexed structured caption and uses this dictionary as an explicit source of queryable evidence. Given a query, TimeTraveler determines whether to recall past evidence, read the present context, or wait for future observations, and then applies a strategy-specific evidence acquisition process. Comparative experiments on streaming video QA benchmarks show that TimeTraveler improves streaming question answering by preserving fine-grained temporal evidence and selecting the appropriate temporal strategy for each query. The project page is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Domain Generalization for Style-Debiased Object Detection via Label-Consistent Training across Multiple Styles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10262)**  `arXiv:2611.10262`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chaoran Zhang, Shirshak Bhusal, Zhilin Gao, Qiang Xue, Haoting Shen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Domain generalization (DG) for object detection aims to improve model performance on unseen target domains. Existing detectors, however, suffer substantial degradation on visually distinct unseen domains, as models confined to limited training styles tend to overfit superficial statistical cues rather than object-level semantics. Motivated by human perception, which attends primarily to semantically relevant features while ignoring irrelevant style cues, we propose Origin-Anchored Multi-Style Consistency (OAMSC), a source-only framework that couples multi-style data synthesis with cross-style consistency learning to suppress style-sensitive shortcut learning. For data preparation, we leverage off-the-shelf diffusion models to expand the source datasets into 20 distinct styles, with all generated images manually verified to ensure that object instances and annotations remain consistent across style variants. During training, we impose Origin-anchored consistency constraints at scene and object levels to reinforce the learning of domain-stable features while suppressing the influence of style-sensitive cues. Extensive experiments and ablation studies on multiple challenging DG benchmarks demonstrate the consistent effectiveness and strong generalization performance of OAMSC across diverse domain shifts. Notably, our approach applies to both one-stage and two-stage detectors, while its data-centric design is complementary in principle to other DG methods. The training code and two style-transfer datasets constructed for the experiments, comprising approximately 180K images in total, are publicly available on GitHub.
  </details>

- **[ATLAS: A Multi-Procedure Benchmark and Context-Aware Model for Surgical Anatomy Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10182)**  `arXiv:2611.10182`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ronald L. P. D. de Jong, Tim J. M. Jaspers, Raf A. H. Vervoort, Aron F. H. A. Bakker, Anna L. Dijk, Yiping Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recognizing anatomical structures during minimally invasive surgery (MIS) could support intraoperative navigation and improve patient safety, but progress is limited by small, single-procedure datasets and by segmentation methods developed for natural scenes. This work addresses both limitations through a new dataset and model. First, we introduce ATLAS-120k, a clip-level semantic segmentation dataset of 121,018 annotated frames from 100 surgical videos, covering 14 laparoscopic and robot-assisted procedures and 41 classes. Annotations were created with a pipeline that combines manual labeling, tracker-based propagation, and review by surgeons. Second, we propose ATLAS (Anatomy Recognition with Context Learning using Foundation Representations), a video semantic segmentation model for surgical anatomy recognition. ATLAS builds on in-domain foundation-model embeddings and adds context queries for the procedure and surgical phase, together with query propagation between frames for short-term memory. ATLAS outperforms state-of-the-art natural-image and in-domain foundation models in both segmentation accuracy and temporal consistency, achieving a Dice score of 0.70 with ViT-L while running in real time at 76.2 FPS. Ablations show that the context queries improve accuracy and that query propagation improves temporal consistency. ATLAS also generalizes well, matching a dedicated model zero-shot on CholecSeg8k and improving phase recognition on AutoLaparo over SurgeNet initialization. The model weights, dataset annotations, and annotation tool are publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Improving the Diffusability of Motion Tokenizer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10160)**  `arXiv:2611.10160`  `cs.CV`  
  _Guanhe Huang, Songqiao Han, Tangzheng Lian, Oya Celiktutan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Latent diffusion models paired with a variational auto-encoder (VAE) tokenizer have shown promising performance for efficient text-driven human motion generation. However, motion latent diffusion suffers from a generation-reconstruction trade-off: simply increasing tokenizer capacity enhances reconstruction but fails to proportionally improve text-conditioned generation quality. We assume this trade-off stems from a spectral mismatch inside the VAE tokenizer: the encoder yields a latent space biased toward high frequencies, and the decoder introduces temporal artifacts. In this paper, we propose the Diffusable Motion Tokenizer (DiMoT) to improve the diffusability of the tokenizer, rather than scaling up the diffusion backbone. To improve text-conditioned optimization, Critic Spectral Adaptation (CSA) leverages a pretrained diffusion model as the critic to suppress high-frequency latent energy. To preserve motion dynamics, the Alpha-Flow Motion Decoder (AMD) performs generative decoding via average-velocity prediction, enabling efficient one-step decoding. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DiMoT achieves state-of-the-art performance across various challenging text-to-motion benchmarks, yielding superior motion fidelity and text-motion alignment. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[VLM as an Editing Pilot: VLM-Steered Velocity Control for Flow-Based Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10066)**  `arXiv:2611.10066`  `cs.CV`  
  _Kuan-Shen Chiu, Jia-Wei Liao, Yu-Chieh Chao, Li-Xuan Peng, Wei-Yun Ma, Cheng-Fu Chou_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-guided image editing requires realizing the requested semantic change while preserving content unrelated to the instruction. FlowEdit provides an efficient inversion-free solution, but its source-anchored coupling can become overly restrictive, causing zero-edit, where the output remains close to the source and the requested change fails to emerge. We find that source coupling directly governs the preservation--editing trade-off, while a fixed coupling schedule cannot adapt to timestep-specific editing needs. Motivated by this observation, we propose V2C-Edit, a training-free VLM-steered velocity control framework. V2C-Edit constructs complementary coupling-controlled velocities and dynamically selects among them using Instruction-Level Similarity (ILS), which evaluates whether the predicted transformation follows the editing instruction. Adaptive spatial masking further preserves regions unrelated to the edit. Experiments on MagicBrush and PIE-Bench with FLUX and Stable Diffusion 3 show that V2C-Edit consistently improves instruction alignment over FlowEdit while maintaining competitive structural preservation, and that ILS better reflects editing effectiveness than target-caption CLIP similarity. Our code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[REFORM-3D: A Representation-Centric Evaluation Framework for 3D Medical Vision Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10062)**  `arXiv:2611.10062`  `cs.CV`  
  _Maregu Assefa, Muzammal Naseer, Divya Velayudhan, Gedamu Alemu Kumie, Naoufel Werghi_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Evaluation of 3D medical foundation models is typically dominated by downstream task scores, which entangle representation quality with adaptation choices. This is especially critical in volumetric imaging, where spacing and anisotropy can substantially alter image statistics while the anatomy itself remains unchanged. We therefore introduce \textbf{REFORM-3D}, a \textbf{R}epresentation-centric \textbf{E}valuation \textbf{F}ramew\textbf{OR}k for benchmarking frozen 3D medical vision foundation \textbf{M}odels across three questions: Acquisition Robustness, asking whether features remain stable when spacing varies while anatomy is fixed; Cross-Modal Anatomical Alignment, asking whether labeled anatomical structures remain recoverable across modalities under balanced bidirectional retrieval; and Anatomical Transfer, asking whether organ identity remains recoverable from limited labeled support under case-disjoint evaluation. These are also pretraining questions, because what a 3D encoder learns depends on how volumes are geometrically presented during self-supervised pretraining. We further introduce Med3DINO encoder family, pretrained on $62.6$K unlabeled MR, CT, PET, CTA, and CBCT volumes under three explicit geometric assumptions: isotropic normalization, voxel-relative sampling, and spacing-aware resampling. Across Med3DINO and heterogeneous external encoders, REFORM-3D reveals module-specific strengths rather than a single model ordering. Within the controlled Med3DINO comparison, voxel-relative sampling yields lower embedding drift, whereas spacing-aware resampling favors anatomical transfer; semantic-transfer advantages depend on the dataset and feature type. Project link: \href{this https URL}{\highlightLR{REFORM-3D}}.
  </details>

- **[Representation Learning for 3D Brain Imaging: A Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10007)**  `arXiv:2611.10007`  `cs.CV`  
  _Pierre Falconnier, Robin Trombetta, Nicolas Duchateau, Carole Lartizien_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Many deep learning architectures for computer vision tasks rely on features extracted by a pre-trained encoder on large databases of natural 2D RGB images. However, the wide domain gap with medical images hampers the direct transfer of optimized deep learning models from computer vision to clinical applications. Recently, self-supervised pre-training and foundation models emerged as promising alternatives to mitigate these limitations. Yet, constructing such models for medical imaging faces critical challenges, including designing and adapting training strategies for large 3D volume datasets, and the need for standardized benchmarks to evaluate feature extraction performance. In this study, we propose an extensive comparison of vision encoders with a focus on 3D brain MRI. First, we consider 8 fully and self-supervised models pretrained on natural images (e.g. ResNet, DINO, CLIP) and 10 backbone architectures trained on medical images, either based on supervised (e.g. RadImageNet) or self-supervised (e.g. BiomedCLIP, SimCLR) tasks. All pre-trained encoders are evaluated on various downstream tasks to assess the representation power of their latent representations, including age and sex prediction as well as disease classification on the ADNI and PPMI datasets. Our study shows that 2D encoders pre-trained on natural images perform the most favorably, even compared to models pre-trained on more than 100k brain MRI volumes, highlighting the need for more suitable and robust methods for representation learning in 3D medical imaging. The source code for this study is publicly available at: https: //github.com/PierreFalconnier/brain-representation-benchmark.
  </details>

- **[MATA: Motion-aligned Temporal Adapter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.10004)**  `arXiv:2611.10004`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ashish Kumar Singh, Prakash Chandra Chhipa_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Few-shot fine-grained video action recognition requires recognizing novel action classes from only a few labeled examples. The difficulty is that visually similar actions often share actors, scenes, poses, and objects, differing only in the order, timing, and short duration of motion changes. We study this problem under three practical constraints: few tunable parameters, low compute cost, and use of the video modality alone. We propose Motion-aligned Temporal Adapter (MATA), a parameter-efficient adapter on top of a frozen CLIP backbone. MATA specializes CLIP features along the temporal dimension using two components: temporal phase alignment through learnable temporal positional embeddings, and a local motion transition operator through depth-wise convolution. With only ~3.6M tunable parameters, MATA achieves state-of-the-art performance across multiple fine-grained benchmarks while remaining robust to both visual corruptions and temporal disruptions induced by frame dropping. Code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[Stochastic Deep Supervision for Gallery-Consistent Early-Exit Face Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09987)**  `arXiv:2611.09987`  `cs.CV`  
  _Guray Ozgur, Tahar Chettaoui, Eduarda Caldeira, Naser Damer, Fadi Boutros_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision Transformers (ViTs) perform strongly in face recognition (FR), yet their fixed-depth inference limits deployment in resource-constrained settings. We propose SD-ViT, a training framework that gives a usable exit at every block depth by making every depth produce discriminative embeddings, for budget-selected fixed-depth exits. Standard ViTs supervise intermediate layers only through backpropagation, so their intermediate representations are not directly optimized to form usable identity embeddings. SD-ViT adds a stochastic auxiliary signal: at each iteration a randomly selected intermediate block is passed through the same embedding head as the final layer and trained with a weighted margin-penalty softmax loss. The objective is thus stochastically sampled deep supervision through a shared classifier. Because both loss terms share that head and classifier, intermediate tokens are trained to map into the same embedding space as final-layer tokens. This matters for FR, where matching is by similarity: a probe processed at an early exit can be compared directly against full-depth gallery embeddings, with no gallery recomputation or adaptation layers. On established benchmarks, SD-ViT stays within 0.4 IJB-C points of the same backbone trained conventionally at full depth, while its early-exit probes recover over 96% of their own full-depth performance at 50% of the computational cost without modifying the reference gallery. A single verification threshold calibrated at full depth remains usable from quarter depth onward.
  </details>

- **[SceneShifter: Training-free Multi-Scene Temporal Control for Audio-driven Human Animation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09960)**  `arXiv:2611.09960`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jianzhi Long, Rong-Cheng Tu, Hao Guan, Siyuan Liang, Shunyu Liu, Xiao Luo, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Although audio-driven human animation has achieved impressive realism, it still lacks effective Multi-Scene Temporal Control: orchestrating background transitions at precise timestamps while preserving a coherent foreground subject. We show that this challenge stems from the diffusion transformer's self-attention mechanism, and addressing it requires two capabilities: (1) Semantic-Aware Temporal Decoupling, which isolates background attention across scenes while retaining cross-scene foreground attention for subject consistency; and (2) Foreground Motion Localization, which accurately tracks the foreground subject across latent frames, especially under large motion. To address these challenges, we introduce SceneShifter, a training-free framework for precise multi-scene control in audio-driven human animation. SceneShifter guides spatio-temporal self-attention by suppressing cross-scene attention among background tokens while preserving cross-scene attention among foreground tokens. To support this guidance under large motion, SceneShifter extracts dynamic foreground masks from the self-attention layers and heads that best capture subject trajectories. We further introduce SceneShifterBench, a benchmark designed to evaluate scene-timing accuracy, foreground preservation, and visual quality under large motion, multiple subjects. Experiments show that SceneShifter achieves frame-level scene timing, strong subject preservation, and high visual quality, outperforming existing baselines. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[MotionCore: A Latent Neural Simulator for Video Generation with Rigid-Body Dynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08904)**  `arXiv:2611.08904`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zheng Wang, Zhengwei Gong, Yuanzhi Liang, Haibin Huang, Chi Zhang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video generation models can produce highly realistic videos while generating physically inconsistent object motion. This limitation suggests that visual modeling alone may not provide a transferable representation of physical dynamics. We introduce MotionCore, a framework for learning physical dynamics via a learnable partial differential equation solver and focuses on rigid-body motion. We introduce a latent neural simulator learning to predict the evolution of object-level physical states from observed motion. Its predictions are then converted into spatially aligned latent controls for a controllable video diffusion model. The generator serves as a visual interface: it provides appearance and scene detail, while the learned dynamics model specifies the physical trajectory. To preserve this trajectory through the interface, MotionCore combines a diffusion-matching objective with a low-capacity decodability constraint on object identity and position. We evaluate MotionCore on public physical-consistency benchmarks and on paired data with known trajectories. Results indicate that MotionCore improves physical consistency and trajectory obedience in the evaluated rigid-body settings, while maintaining comparable visual quality. A controlled ablation protocol compares learned dynamics with an analytic prior and studies the choice of latent evolution processor.
  </details>

- **[SplatAnything: Gaussian Spacing Normalization for Feed-forward 3DGS across Arbitrary Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.08264)**  `arXiv:2611.08264`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hao Yu, Zhenwei Wang, Yongzhen Hu, Yuning Gong, Junta Wu, Xiaowei Zhou, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We identify a key limitation of existing feed-forward 3DGS methods: Gaussian attributes are typically predicted in a radial-distance-normalized space that canonicalizes a global radial statistic of the reconstructed geometry, while the spacing between neighboring Gaussian centers can still vary substantially across scene scales and camera configurations. This mismatch induces systematic shifts in Gaussian-scale targets, leading to ambiguous supervision. To address this, we propose SplatAnything with Gaussian Spacing Normalization (GSNorm), which canonicalizes a robust scene-level statistic of Gaussian spacing to provide a more consistent spatial reference for Gaussian prediction. To rigorously assess our method, we further establish a systematic benchmark spanning diverse datasets, input sparsity levels, and challenging extrapolated viewpoints. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SplatAnything achieves state-of-the-art performance, with particularly strong gains under substantial variations in scene scale and viewpoint. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[OCRia-1 Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00092)**  `arXiv:2611.00092`  `cs.CV`  
  _Aman Rangapur, Antonio Jimeno Yepes, Daniel Bustamante, Nutchapol Dendumrongsup, Sebastián G. Acosta, Pierre-Loïc Doulcet, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  We introduce OCRia-1, a 4B vision-language model (VLM) for document parsing that excels at preserving table structure, extracting chart data and retaining text formatting. OCRia-1 handles content that has traditionally been challenging in document parsing: merged cells and spanning headers, tables with thousands of cells, chart data, and meaningful text formatting such as bold, italics, subscripts and superscripts, preserving details needed for business-critical document analysis. At the time of this report, it achieves the highest scores on tables, charts and text formatting among the systems evaluated, including a 35B model and Mistral OCR 4.1. Its mean ParseBench score over the four dimensions we evaluate is 0.87, against 0.75 for the next-best system.We emphasize data quality and reward design in training OCRia-1. Synthetic pages provide exact ground truth for long tables and rich formatting, while real pages are transcribed using a pipeline grounded in the PDF text layer. We attribute OCRia-1's performance primarily to careful data curation that prioritizes quality and diversity over quantity. Dimension-specific rewards evaluate table reconstruction, chart-data extraction and text-formatting fidelity. We train the model on the curated data using supervised fine-tuning (SFT), then refine it through reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR).To our knowledge, OCRia-1 is the fastest published document-parsing VLM in its class, decoding at about 4,700 tokens per second with a single request on one B200 GPU, about nine times faster than the base model on stock vLLM. This is achieved through a block-diffusion speculator, quantization and custom kernels.
  </details>

- **[A Unified Benchmark for Aerial-Enhanced Autonomous Driving: The KITScenes Aerial Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.40455)**  `arXiv:2610.40455`  `cs.CV`  
  _Daniel Lengerer, Richard Schwarzkopf, Jonas Merkert, Fabian Immel, Rupert Polley, Frank Bieder, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Aerial imagery provides complementary spatial context for autonomous driving by exposing road structure beyond the immediate perception range of onboard sensors. Yet map construction from aerial imagery, aerial-assisted online HD mapping, and cross-view localization are typically studied on separate data and benchmarks. We introduce KITScenes Aerial, an extension of the KITScenes Multimodal dataset that augments synchronized onboard sensor data, vehicle poses, and rich Lanelet2 map annotations with spatially associated aerial imagery and higher-resolution neural representations, providing a common basis for a variety of research tasks. Our mapping evaluation focuses on all 53 KITScenes map classes across different model designs, spatial ranges, and aerial resolutions, while retaining compatibility with the widely used three-class setting. Representative baselines show that aerial context is particularly beneficial for fine-grained and extended-range mapping, more than doubling 53-class mAP at the larger mapping extent. Higher-resolution aerial imagery yields a further 4.3 mAP (+24 %) improvement, with gains extending to challenging map elements such as traffic lights. We additionally evaluate cross-view localization under geographically disjoint conditions, where an adapted fine-grained feature-matching baseline achieves a mean translation error of 5.13 m. KITScenes Aerial thus enables unified study of aerial and ground-level information across complementary mapping and localization tasks. Project page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[MVRCap: Towards Comprehensive Video Captioning via Hierarchical Multi-Dimensional Verifiable Rewards](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.36838)**  `arXiv:2610.36838`  `cs.CV`  
  _Weihuang Lin, Xianing Chen, Zhibin Wang, Congzhi Zhang, Yiwei Ma, Jiayi Ji, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Despite significant progress, current models struggle to generate comprehensive video captions, constrained primarily by coarse-grained supervision, which often fails to capture spatial-temporal dynamics and the underlying logical intent. To address this issue, we introduce MVRCap, a novel framework that reformulates video captioning as a reinforcement learning task steered by structured verification. Central to our approach is a Fine-Grained Verifiable Reward mechanism, serving as dense supervision to enforce rigorous visual-semantic alignment. Specifically, we construct this reward system anchored by three complementary pillars: a Hierarchical Concept Coverage Reward utilizing our newly constructed MVRCap-QA dataset to ensure multi-dimensional completeness and semantic depth, a Factuality Verification Reward to enhance factual consistency, and a Conciseness Constraint to maximize semantic density. Furthermore, we curate the high-quality MVRCap-SFT dataset, providing structured annotations across six key dimensions, to serve as a robust foundational initialization for the RL policy in this training pipeline. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MVRCap achieves state-of-the-art performance on authoritative benchmarks including VDC, CAPability, and VC4VG.
  </details>

- **[GIFT: Representation Geometry Matters for Single-Domain Generalized Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35374)**  `arXiv:2610.35374`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yin Zhang, Yaoyue Zheng, Yongqiang Zhang, Bogdan Raducanu, Dan Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Single-Domain Generalized Object Detection (S-DGOD) aims to generalize from a single source domain to unseen domains under distribution shifts. Existing efforts mainly focus on simulating unseen domains or suppressing domain-specific factors. However, they largely overlook the intrinsic generalization capability embedded in Vision Foundation Models (VFMs). In this work, we show that unconstrained fine-tuning disrupts the geometric structure of pre-trained representations, which encodes domain-invariant semantics. Motivated by this, we propose Geometric-Invariance Fine-Tuning (GIFT), a geometric-aware fine-tuning method for VFMs. Specifically, we introduce a series of learnable Householder reflections to the pre-trained weights to encourage geometry-consistent adaptation under domain shift. In addition, we propose a lightweight channel-wise scaling to facilitate effective task-specific adaptation. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GIFT consistently outperforms state-of-the-art (SOTA) approaches on two S-DGOD benchmarks. Notably, by introducing fewer than 1% additional trainable parameters into the frozen backbone, the proposed method achieves improvements of +7.9% and +2.9% in mPC over the previous SOTA on the Cityscapes-C and Diverse Weather Dataset, respectively. This finding highlights that preserving representation geometry is crucial for robust cross-domain generalization, establishing GIFT as a principled and efficient solution to domain shift.
  </details>

- **[Understanding and Repairing Cooperative Perception under Adverse Weather](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35110)**  `arXiv:2610.35110`  `cs.CV`  
  _Mengxu Ma, Linshan Yan, Kailong Zhang, Ruiyang Guo, Dagang Shen, Yichen Fan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  A cooperative detector can lose a correct vehicle detection when surrounding LiDAR measurements change, even if the returns on the vehicle remain unchanged. We study this failure as Weather-Induced Context Interference. On our CoInfra development set, controlled removal outside annotated object boxes turns 22.1% of V2X-ViT's previously correct detections into misses. Restoring network features around the objects to their values before removal recovers 98.6% of these misses. The same restoration in each of three equally sized regions excluding the objects recovers none. Candidate analysis finds accurate boxes that are not selected, while other targets lack well-localized candidates. With paired reference features unavailable at inference, Adverse-Weather Cooperative Repair (AWCR) repairs candidates from current observations. A lightweight Local Correction head generates bounded alternatives, and Quality Selection ranks them alongside original boxes with a correction weight. In V2X-R simulation, AWCR also filters peer returns before detection using Point Classifier trained on generated-point labels. Candidate repair improves mean car BEV AP@0.7 by 4.22 points across three fits on natural adverse-weather CoInfra development inputs and vehicle AP@0.7 by 5.00 points on a rain-affected public recording. It also improves released HEAL, FadeLead, and V2X-DGW detectors with their pretrained weights fixed. On the full V2X-Radar-C validation benchmark, HEAL with AWCR achieves state-of-the-art LiDAR-only cooperative detection performance of 68.22 vehicle BEV AP@0.7.
  </details>

- **[Ask About the Right Moment: Training-Free Object Identity Reasoning for Video-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31471)**  `arXiv:2610.31471`  `cs.CV`  
  _Di Yang, Xiaochang Li, Muyang Liu, Yufei Wang, Yanhai Xiong_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Many questions about a video concern one physical object. Where does the ball end up after the cups are shuffled? Which card is the queen? How often was the mug covered? Open video-language models (Video-LMs) recognise such objects in single frames but do not keep them apart over time. On shuffled shell games, open models from eight families stay near chance, and so do published tool agents. We separate semantic identity binding from temporal identity maintenance. Binding should happen at a moment when the evidence for the object is visible, and maintenance should be left to a tracker. Track, Then Bind implements this principle without training. A promptable video tracker follows every candidate object and draws it with a track number. A frozen vision-language model (VLM) answers one question about the identifying evidence, which binds the target to a track. The answer is then derived from a text description of the tracks. With Qwen3-VL-32B, the agent solves both VET-Bench tasks, where a fine-tuned trajectory model reaches 92% and 90%. On real cup games from the Perception Test, it raises accuracy from 35.5% to 52.3%. Without retuning, the same configuration also solves VET-Bench with Gemma 4 and GPT-5.5. Its track-derived visibility timelines add 8.8-17.9 points on TOC-Bench for open models. GPT-6.1 already answers 92.5% of the real cup games directly but 71.0% through our pipeline, while our timelines still raise its TOC-Bench accuracy from 52.5% to 57.7%. The benefit of external tracking thus depends on the underlying model and the task.
  </details>

- **[Lightweight Frame Compensation for Real-Time Polyp Segmentation under Frame Skipping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31228)**  `arXiv:2610.31228`  `cs.CV`  
  _Weihao Gao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Real-time polyp segmentation in computer-assisted colonoscopy is hampered by the difficulty of deploying high-accuracy models under strict latency budgets. Frame skipping, which performs inference every K frames and reuses or interpolates the rest, improves throughput but introduces motion blur and lesion misalignment that degrade temporal consistency. To close this gap, we propose the Lightweight Frame Compensator (LFC), a 77K-parameter plug-and-play module inserted before a frozen segmentation backbone. Operating on a three-frame window, LFC fuses consecutive frames via spatio-temporal attention into a single compensated frame, and is trained only on pseudo-labels generated by a differently seeded model, without any expert annotation. Experiments on three polyp video benchmarks show that LFC recovers most of the accuracy degraded by direct frame skipping and, in several settings, reaches the accuracy of frame-by-frame inference, while retaining a 2.05X--2.55X throughput gain. We further evaluate the temporal stability of three backbones spanning CNN, pyramid Transformer, and global-attention Transformer architectures, revealing a spectrum of sensitivity to frame skipping that arises from how locally or globally each architecture aggregates misaligned input. The compensation gain of LFC follows this spectrum: it is largest for high-capacity Transformer backbones, which achieve the best static accuracy but suffer the most under skip-frame inference. LFC thus offers a lightweight, pluggable, and deployment-friendly route to real-time polyp segmentation.
  </details>

- **[EvaOmni: An Evidence-Grounded Benchmark for Audio-Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.28444)**  `arXiv:2610.28444`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiaoxing Hu, Wenxi Yao, Chaoyuan Shi, Ziqi Ye, Xiaorong Guo, Peixuan Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Omni LLMs can naturally locate key audio and visual evidence and integrate information across modalities for audiovisual reasoning. Existing audio-visual reasoning benchmarks that mainly rely on multiple-choice questions usually score models solely based on answer correctness, making it difficult to assess whether a model truly forms a reasoning chain from evidence to the correct answer. In addition, textual or unimodal shortcuts may allow models to answer correctly without analyzing the necessary audio-visual evidence, further weakening the reliability of evaluation benchmarks. To address these issues, we introduce EvaOmni, an evidence-grounded benchmark containing 392 videos and 1,027 purely human-annotated question-answer pairs, covering 15 capabilities and three levels: retrieval and localization, basic understanding, and complex reasoning. We design a series of rigorous annotation procedures in which experts annotate audio and visual evidence and construct video-based distractors; humans review whether questions meet strict inspection criteria, and finally models filter textual and unimodal shortcuts. Beyond high-quality question-answer pairs, we require models to simultaneously return the answer and supporting evidence intervals in order to evaluate the complete reasoning chain. We propose the Coverage Grounded Score (C.G.) to measure how much annotated evidence a model identifies, and the Bottleneck Grounded Score (B.G.) to penalize poor temporal localization in either modality; both are incorporated into answer correctness. Across 7 open-weight and 17 proprietary Omni LLMs, Gemini3.7-Flash achieved the highest overall accuracy (79.4%), C.G. (70.8%), and B.G. (35.1%). At Level 3, it reached 79.1% accuracy, but its B.G. was only 12.3%. These results show that even the most advanced models still have room for development in grounded audio-visual reasoning.
  </details>

- **[ContraSafe-VL: A Contrastive Safety Preference Dataset for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23908)**  `arXiv:2610.23908`  `cs.CV`  
  _Di Wu, Yanyan Zhao, Xin Lu, Hao Yang, Taotao Zhang, Yunzhen Zhao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-Language Models (VLMs) are increasingly deployed as general-purpose multimodal assistants, making multimodal safety alignment an important challenge. Safety depends on the interplay between textual and visual content, with visual context often playing a critical role. However, VLMs may fail to use visual information appropriately, either overlooking safety-relevant visual content or over-interpreting sensitive visual content, leading to unsafe responses or over-refusal. Existing multimodal safety alignment datasets largely follow the response-preference paradigm, which teaches models how to respond but provides limited supervision on how visual context should inform the safety judgment, making it difficult to balance safety and over-refusal. To address this limitation, we introduce \textbf{ContraSafe-VL}, a \textbf{Contra}stive \textbf{Safe}ty preference dataset for \textbf{V}ision-\textbf{L}anguage Models. For each fixed textual query, ContraSafe-VL constructs paired visual contexts that result in multimodal queries with different safety labels, providing direct supervision for how visual context should inform multimodal safety judgments. It further provides response preference pairs based on harmlessness and helpfulness to encourage models to generate responses that are both safe and helpful. Experiments across three VLM architectures and multiple benchmarks demonstrate that training with ContraSafe-VL improves cross-modal safety recognition and achieves a better safety--over-refusal trade-off while preserving general multimodal capabilities. The dataset is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[CitySTAR: Structured and Topology-Aware Reasoning for Open-Vocabulary Urban 3D Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.19911)**  `arXiv:2609.19911`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuai Zhang, Hongye Hou, Qinghe Liu, Zhuoxiao Li, Dongli Wu, Jing Ou, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D grounding aims to localize target entities in complex scenes from natural language and plays a fundamental role in embodied perception and spatial reasoning. However, existing approaches mostly rely on feature similarity or direct matching, making it difficult to connect natural-language intent with the implicit semantic and geometric structures hidden in billion-scale urban point clouds. We reformulate city-scale 3D grounding as structured constraint reasoning, where description semantics are organized into computable cross-modal constraints over open-vocabulary 3D entities, attributes, and spatial relations. We present CitySTAR, a training-free framework for reasoning-driven urban 3D grounding. CitySTAR lifts raw billion-scale urban point clouds into a query-ready scene graph of open-vocabulary 3D instances, with CodeLLM-driven tools supplying multimodal evidence for node attributes and 3D spatial relations. It then models target-context topology with paired hypergraphs and performs bidirectional topology verification for structural disambiguation. Finally, a Reflective Cross-modal Grounding module integrates topology consistency and candidate-centered 2D visual evidence to make decisions over a metric-aware 3D context graph. To further support this setting, we introduce CitySTAR-3D, an enhanced benchmark that improves semantic coverage, instance completeness, bounding-box fidelity, and spatial-relation complexity in city-scale 3D grounding. Extensive experiments show that CitySTAR consistently improves open-world urban 3D grounding while maintaining strong interpretability and generalization.
  </details>

- **[From Detection to Understanding: TAR and TAR-Bench for Multi-Task Traffic Anomaly Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10317)**  `arXiv:2608.10317`  `cs.CV`  
  _Han Zhang, Yilin Zhao, Zaid Pervaiz Bhat, Zheng Tang, Varun Praveen, Vidya N. Murali, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Detecting a traffic anomaly does not establish whether a video-language model can explain what happened, localize it in time, or identify its causes. We introduce TAR (Traffic Anomaly Reasoning) and TAR-Bench, paired resources for training and evaluating these complementary capabilities across 10 tasks spanning question answering, temporal reasoning, and scene understanding. TAR contains 44,040 automatically generated annotations with chain-of-thought traces for 3,670 CCTV videos from eight public datasets. TAR-Bench provides 960 human-curated annotations for 80 held-out clips from 17 public YouTube videos. Evaluation of eleven vision-language models and analysis of fine-tuned outputs reveal temporal, causal, and descriptive errors not captured by closed-form question-answering accuracy. Training on all 10 tasks raises mean benchmark scores from 34.3 to 55.7 for Cosmos-Reason2-8B and from 30.9 to 53.9 for Qwen3-VL-8B-Instruct. These results support joint training across complementary tasks, while highlighting persistent limitations in temporal precision and causal attribution. TAR and TAR-Bench serve as the official training and in-domain evaluation resources for AI City Challenge 2026 Track~3. The dataset is available under CC-BY-4.0 atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Searching Videos as Trees: Self-Correcting Agents for Grounded Long Video QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.16189)**  `arXiv:2607.16189`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ce Zhang, Ziyang Wang, Yulu Pan, Oluwatumininu Oguntola, Pranav Wagh, Qiyu Wu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Grounded long-video question answering (Grounded LVQA) requires answering a question about a long video while localizing the short evidence interval that supports the answer. Recent agentic methods frame this task as multi-turn exploration with a single crop_video(start, end) action, which supports coarse-to-fine narrowing but provides no primitive for fine-to-coarse backtracking. As a result, these agents typically converge prematurely and cannot recover from an early mistake. We propose VideoTreeSearch (VTS), a framework that casts grounded LVQA as iterative self-correcting search over an adaptive temporal tree. VTS constructs a non-uniform tree through adaptive temporal segmentation so that each node corresponds to a semantically coherent segment, and trains an agent to navigate the tree through four discrete operations: zoom_in, zoom_out, shift, and answer. These operations expose backtracking and recovery as explicit, learnable primitives rather than implicit behaviors. To train this navigation, we introduce a trajectory synthesis pipeline that produces multi-step paths through the tree, including deliberate detours into incorrect branches followed by recovery. We use these trajectories for supervised fine-tuning, followed by reinforcement learning with grounding and answer-accuracy rewards. On three Grounded LVQA benchmarks (CG-Bench, Haystack-LVBench, Haystack-Ego4D), VTS outperforms the strongest prior multi-turn cropping agents by +12.5 mIoU on CG-Bench and +7.4 T-F1 on Haystack-Ego4D. The learned policy also transfers to general long-video QA, surpassing all prior multi-turn cropping agents on Video-MME, MLVU, and LVBench by up to +7.1 accuracy points. Ablations confirm that self-correcting hierarchical search is the central mechanism behind these gains: removing either adaptive descent or explicit backtracking substantially degrades performance.
  </details>

- **[FOLIO: Focused Semantic Memory for Streaming Video Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13298)**  `arXiv:2607.13298`  `cs.CV`  
  _Haoyang Fan, Dhruv Parikh, Anvitha Ramachandran, Sameh Gobriel, Nilesh Jain, Rajgopal Kannan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In online streaming video understanding, a video stream continues to arrive and queries may be issued at any time. Because streaming frames grow without bound, the system must continuously compress and retain information from the observed video prefix while future frames and future queries remain unknown. The core challenge is deciding what information to retain and how to organize the maintained history: as this history grows with the stream, memory cost increases and many redundant visual details are retained, whereas later queries often depend on specific entities, actions, and their temporal changes. To address this challenge, we introduce FOLIO, a training-free focused semantic memory system that records important parts of the stream in higher detail while keeping surrounding context compact. As the stream arrives, FOLIO updates memory at the segment level, guided by a dynamic focus state, combining a short-term visual buffer with a long-term semantic memory organized around observed entities and linked to a visual-evidence cache. At query time, lightweight hybrid retrieval combines direct matching over the structured memory with semantic query expansion. FOLIO achieves state-of-the-art performance, reaching 82.0/69.1 Perception/Backward accuracy on OVO-Bench with Qwen3-VL-8B and 74.5 overall accuracy on StreamingBench, while substantially reducing the cost of maintaining streaming memory by reserving detailed records for focused entities and storing surrounding context compactly.
  </details>

- **[Read It Back: Pretrained MLLMs Are Zero-Shot Reward Models for Text-to-Image Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.11886)**  `arXiv:2607.11886`  `cs.CV`  
  _Runhui Huang, Qihui Zhang, Zhe Liu, Yu Gao, Jie Wu, Hengshuang Zhao_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In this paper, we propose SpectraReward, a training-free reward function that turns pretrained MLLMs into off-the-shelf reward models for image-generation reinforcement learning. Instead of asking the MLLM to judge a generated image or answer decomposed verification questions, SpectraReward measures how well the original prompt can be recovered from the generated image through a single image-conditioned, teacher-forced forward pass. We use the average image-conditioned prompt log-likelihood as the reward, directly reusing the MLLM's pretrained image-text alignment ability without preference labels, reward-model fine-tuning. We further introduce Self-SpectraReward, a special case for unified multimodal models where the policy's own understanding branch serves as the reward model for its generation branch, forming a closed-loop self-improving framework without external reward models or external knowledge. Extensive experiments validate SpectraReward through a broad image-generation RL study covering two diffusion models, three RL algorithms, nine reward MLLM backbones from four MLLM families spanning 4B to 235B parameters, and five out-of-distribution text-to-image benchmarks. Results show that both SpectraReward and Self-SpectraReward significantly and consistently improve generation performance and outperform prior MLLM-derived reward training methods. Further analysis reveals that larger reward MLLMs are not always better, while Self-SpectraReward can match or surpass much larger external reward models, suggesting that reward-policy alignment is a key factor for effective image-generation RL. Project Page:this https URL
  </details>

- **[RedPhys: An Extensible Red-Teaming Platform for Physical-World Models and Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23892)**  `arXiv:2606.23892`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yifei Zhao, Qian Lou, Mengxin Zheng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Physical-world models (PhyModels) perform grounded perception, reasoning, or decision tasks; physical-world agents (PhyAgents) combine these models with planning or tool use. We introduce RedPhys, an extensible red-teaming platform, instantiated here with vision-language models and agents. Shared interfaces make tasks, test methods, defenses, and victim systems configurable and extensible, producing robustness reports and datasets for future alignment. The platform emphasizes adversarial testing that seeks physically grounded functional failures, while also supporting non-adversarial input perturbations without an attack objective. The benchmark integrates 12 red-teaming methods, 3 model-agnostic defenses, and 13 PhyModels across seven physical domains under a practical black-box threat model. An agentic target-generation pipeline derives a scene-specific, physically grounded target, so targeted methods pursue the same intended error. Our evaluation shows that when attackers control the text prompt or add auxiliary visual instructions, injection exposes substantial instruction-following vulnerabilities; among bounded visual-transfer attacks, multimodal co-optimization transfers best; single-pass generative attacks approach iterative methods without per-image optimization; and neither model scale nor physical-reasoning post-training confers robustness. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Tac-DINO: Learning Tactile Features with Patch Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.12069)**  `arXiv:2606.12069`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hong Li, Yankang Dong, Yue Xu, Yihan Tang, Jiamin Qiu, Qihang Yao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multisensory perception is essential for robotic manipulation, with tactile sensing providing critical information for fine-grained, contact-rich interactions. Although prior work integrates tactile and spatial signals to improve perception, vision-tactile learning commonly relies on object-level alignment. Addressing this limitation requires systematically aligned 3D vision-tactile data. We develop a data collection system and introduce Touch3D, a dataset comprising 505 real-world objects and 20,025 tactile contacts. Building on this dataset, we establish the Contact-Centric Multimodal Tactile Representation Benchmark (CoMT-Bench), which expands evaluation from object-level retrieval and tactile properties to include patch-level contact evaluation. We further propose Geometry-Relation Distilled Tactile-Centric Cross-Modal Learning (GeoTAC), which explores tactile learning across all multisensory data. In experiments on the Touch3D test set, patch-level alignment improves the average score by 5.32 over object-level alignment, with GeoTAC's geometric supervision improving it by a further 4.48. In robotic manipulation, GeoTAC improves TP by 19.14 and 11.34 over DINOv2 (Vision-only) and DINOv2 (Vision-Tactile), respectively. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Next Forcing: World Action Modeling with Multi-Chunk Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.11187)**  `arXiv:2606.11187`  `cs.CV`  
  _Gangwei Xu, Qihang Zhang, Jiaming Zhou, Xing Zhu, Yujun Shen, Xin Yang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Autoregressive video generation has emerged as a powerful paradigm for World Action Models (WAMs). However, existing approaches suffer from slow training convergence and limited converged accuracy, particularly at high frame rates, as the training supervision is confined to the current chunk without explicit signals about future dynamics; they also suffer from slow inference due to iterative video denoising. In this paper, we present Next Forcing, a multi-chunk prediction (MCP) framework for world action modeling that enables faster training, higher accuracy, and accelerated inference. Inspired by multi-token prediction in large language models, Next Forcing introduces an MCP training objective that augments the main model with lightweight auxiliary MCP modules to simultaneously denoise video chunks at multiple future temporal horizons (next$^1$, next$^2$, next$^3$ chunks). These MCP modules form a causal chain across prediction depths, where intermediate features fused from multiple layers of the main model are leveraged to predict future dynamics, allowing near-future predictions to inform farther-future ones and providing dense multi-scale temporal supervision back to the main model. During training, the MCP modules significantly accelerate convergence and improve converged accuracy, especially at high frame rates: at 50 fps, Next Forcing achieves a 93.1% relative improvement over LingBot-VA at 5k training steps and 2.3$\times$ faster convergence, and establishes new state-of-the-art results on the RoboTwin benchmark (94.1/93.5% on Clean/Random). At inference, the MCP modules can be retained to predict the next video chunk in parallel with the current one, achieving 2$\times$ inference acceleration. Beyond World Action Models, Next Forcing also improves physical-law adherence on PhyWorld and reduces FVD by over 50\% in action-free general video pretraining.
  </details>

- **[Facial Expression Recognition in the Deep Learning Era: A Systematic Multi-Criteria Review of Methods, Models, Datasets, Performance, Challenges, and Future Research Directions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.08612)**  `arXiv:2606.08612`  `cs.CV`  
  _Spyridon Georgiou, Aggelos Psiris, Spyridon Evangelatos, Thomas Lagkas, Vasileios Argyriou, Panagiotis Sarigiannidis, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Facial Expression Recognition (FER) has advanced rapidly over the last decade, driven by the shift from handcrafted descriptors and shallow classifiers to deep convolutional, attention-based, vision-language, and foundation-model architectures, and by the parallel growth of large-scale in-the-wild benchmarks spanning categorical, dimensional, compound, micro-expression, Action Unit (AU), and intensity-estimation tasks. Yet the deep learning-based FER landscape has so far been reviewed only along narrow task-, architecture-, or application-specific axes, leaving a holistic, systematically organized account of its recent advances missing. This survey addresses that gap with a comprehensive review of recent deep learning-based FER, explicitly linked to the wider Facial Affect Recognition (FAR) domain. Its main contributions are: a) A description of FER's evolution into five distinct phases, from handcrafted features and classical machine learning to attention-based, vision-language, and foundation-model approaches, with the key milestone works of each, b) A multi-criteria taxonomy analyzing the literature along seven complementary axes: recognition task, input modality, face pre-processing pipeline, network architecture, learning strategy, acquisition setting, and application domain, c) A per-criterion comparative analysis, with critical insights into the strengths and limitations of each category under in-the-wild conditions, d) A task-organized review of public FER datasets, with their annotation schemes, modalities, and evaluation protocols, e) A compilation of performance metrics and a per-task quantitative comparison of representative state-of-the-art methods on widely adopted benchmarks, and f) A discussion of current challenges and promising future directions.
  </details>

- **[OmniGF: A Dual-Branch Vision-Language Framework for Unified Gaze Following](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.26399)**  `arXiv:2605.26399`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qiaomu Miao, Haoyu Wu, Jingyi Xu, Minh Hoai, Dimitris Samaras_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Understanding human gaze behavior is essential for complex scene comprehension and human-computer interaction. Traditional gaze following models are typically restricted to pure spatial localization, lacking the high-level capacity to reason about semantic targets or complex social contexts. Furthermore, these models often process individuals sequentially, requiring redundant computations over the same scene image for multi-person inference. While recent Vision-Language Models (VLMs) offer the exceptional semantic reasoning needed to address gaze-related semantic tasks, their reliance on discrete text generation inherently limits precision in continuous spatial tasks like gaze localization. To bridge this gap, we propose OmniGF, a unified vision-language framework that adapts foundational VLMs for highly scalable multi-person gaze reasoning. The model adopts a dual-branch decoding strategy: a structured language branch generates discrete reasoning states, while a continuous spatial branch directly taps into the VLM's dense hidden states. Supervising these extracted representations with high-resolution gaze target heatmaps effectively overcomes the spatial bottleneck of text-only coordinate generation. Furthermore, to explicitly ground the model in multi-person scenes, we augment the input with head embeddings encoded from cropped head images, providing fine-grained appearance and orientation cues for all individuals simultaneously. By modeling all individuals and leveraging the strong semantic capability of VLMs, OmniGF seamlessly integrates precise spatial gaze target estimation, semantic gaze prediction, and complex social gaze reasoning. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our framework establishes new state-of-the-art performance across multiple standard benchmarks. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[A Causally Grounded Taxonomy for Image Degradation Robustness Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.15906)**  `arXiv:2605.15906`  `cs.CV`  
  _Stefan Becker, Simon Weiss, Wolfgang Hübner, Michael Arens_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Image degradations arise throughout acquisition, processing, and transmission, altering both visual appearance and downstream machine-perception performance. They are studied across synthetic corruption benchmarks, perceptual image quality assessment (IQA), low-level vision, and physically grounded imaging-system analysis. However, these communities rely on incompatible grouping schemes and backend-specific severity definitions, making results difficult to compare across datasets, degradation models, and tasks. We propose a causally grounded framework for organizing and interpreting image degradations. Rather than introducing new degradations or redefining existing benchmarks, we provide an interpretive representation and measurement layer that makes implicit assumptions explicit. Each degradation is characterized by two orthogonal axes: its dominant causal source in the imaging pipeline and its dominant perceptual effect. This dual-axis abstraction yields a compact taxonomy spanning algorithmic corruptions, perceptual distortions, low-level imaging artifacts, and physically motivated camera or system failures. To make severity semantics observable without modifying existing implementations, we quantify each native severity level using full-reference image quality metrics: PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS. This separates causal origin, perceptual appearance, measured distortion strength, and downstream task impact while preserving native parameterizations. We demonstrate the framework through COCO-Degradation, a taxonomy-aligned benchmark for evaluating object detector robustness under diverse low-level imaging conditions. By connecting causal source, perceptual effect, and measured severity, the proposed framework supports more interpretable and reproducible evaluation of how low-level visual degradations affect machine perception.
  </details>

- **[PointForward: Feedforward Driving Reconstruction through Point-Aligned Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.11594)**  `arXiv:2605.11594`  `cs.CV`  
  _Cheng Chi, Xianqi Wang, Hongcheng Luo, Mingfei Tu, Gangwei Xu, Zehan Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  High-fidelity reconstruction of driving scenes is crucial for autonomous driving. While recent feedforward 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) methods enable fast reconstruction, their per-pixel Gaussian prediction paradigm often suffers from multi-view inconsistency and layering artifacts. Moreover, existing methods often model dynamic instances via dense flow prediction, which lacks explicit cross-view correspondence and instance-level consistency. In this paper, we propose PointForward, a feedforward driving reconstruction framework through point-aligned representations. Unlike pixel-aligned methods, we initialize sparse 3D queries in world space and aggregate multi-view image information via spatial-temporal fusion onto these queries, enforcing explicit cross-view consistency in a single feedforward pass. To handle scene dynamics, we introduce scene graphs that explicitly organize moving instances during reconstruction. By leveraging 3D bounding boxes, our method enables instance-level motion propagation and temporally consistent dynamic representations. Extensive experiments show that PointForward achieves state-of-the-art performance on large-scale driving benchmarks.
  </details>

- **[FluxFlow: Conservative Flow-Matching for Astronomical Image Super-Resolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.03749)**  `arXiv:2605.03749`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuhong Liu, Xining Ge, Quanfeng Xu, Ziteng Cui, Liuzhuozheng Li, Gengjia Chang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Ground-to-space astronomical super-resolution requires recovering space-quality images from ground-based observations that are simultaneously limited by pixel sampling resolution and atmospheric seeing, which imposes a stochastic, spatially varying PSF that cannot be resolved through upsampling alone. Existing methods rely on synthetic training pairs that fail to capture real atmospheric statistics and are prone to either over-smoothed reconstructions or hallucination sources with no physical counterpart in the observed sky. We propose FluxFlow, a conservative pixel-space flow-matching framework that incorporates observation uncertainty and source-region importance weights during training, and a training-free Wiener-regularized test-time correction to suppress hallucination sources while preserving recovered detail. We further construct the DESI--HST Dataset, the large-scale real-world benchmark comprising 19,500 real co-registered ground-to-space image pairs with real atmospheric PSF variation. Experiments demonstrate that FluxFlow consistently outperforms existing baseline methods in both photometric and scientific accuracy. Project webpage:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Fundus-R1: Training a Fundus-Reading MLLM on Public Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.08322)**  `arXiv:2604.08322`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuchuan Deng, Qijie Wei, Kaiheng Qian, Jiazhen Liu, Bangxiang Lan, Qingwei Shen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Fundus imaging such as CFP, OCT and SLO is crucial for the early detection of retinal anomalies and diseases. Fundus image understanding, due to its knowledge-intensive nature, poses a challenging vision-language task. An emerging approach to addressing the task is to post-train a generic multimodal large language model (MLLM), either by supervised finetuning (SFT) or by reinforcement learning with verifiable rewards (RLVR), on a considerable amount of in-house samples paired with high-quality clinical reports. However, these valuable samples are not publicly accessible, which not only hinders reproducibility but also practically limits research to few players. In this paper, we make a novel attempt to train a reasoning-enhanced fundus-reading MLLM, termed Fundus-R1, using exclusively public datasets, wherein over 94% of the data are annotated with image-level labels only. Our technical contributions are two-fold. First, we propose a RAG-based method for composing image-specific, knowledge-aware reasoning traces. Such auto-generated traces link visual findings identified by a generic MLLM to the image labels in terms of ophthalmic knowledge. Second, we enhance RLVR with a process reward that encourages self-consistency of the generated reasoning trace in each rollout. Extensive experiments on three fundus-reading benchmarks, i.e., FunBench, Omni-Fundus and GMAI-Fundus, show that Fundus-R1 clearly outperforms multiple baselines, including its generic counterpart (Qwen2.5-VL) and a stronger edition post-trained without using the generated traces. This work paves the way for training powerful fundus-reading MLLMs with publicly available data.
  </details>

- **[Stitch4D: 4D Urban Reconstruction from Sparse Multi-Location Observations via Spatio-Temporal Interpolation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.07923)**  `arXiv:2604.07923`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hina Kogure, Kei Katsumata, Taiki Miyanishi, Komei Sugiura_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Dynamic urban environments are often captured by cameras placed at spatially separated locations with little or no view overlap. However, most existing 4D reconstruction methods assume densely overlapping views and struggle under sparse multi-location observations, producing unstable reconstructions in unobserved intermediate regions. To address this practical yet underexplored setting, we propose Stitch4D, a unified 4D reconstruction framework that compensates for missing spatial coverage in sparsely observed urban environments. Stitch4D synthesizes intermediate \emph{bridge views} between distant camera locations and jointly optimizes real and synthesized observations in a unified coordinate frame with inter-location consistency constraints. By recovering intermediate spatial coverage before optimization, Stitch4D mitigates geometric collapse and improves reconstruction stability in sparse regions. To evaluate this setting, we introduce Urban Sparse 4D (U-S4D), a controlled CARLA-based benchmark for free-viewpoint reconstruction under sparse multi-location configurations. Experiments on U-S4D show that Stitch4D consistently outperforms representative 4D reconstruction baselines in image-quality metrics. These results suggest that recovering intermediate spatial coverage is an effective strategy for stabilizing 4D reconstruction in sparse urban environments. The project page is provided inthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Video Object Segmentation under Aquatic Domain Shift: A Diagnostic Benchmark and Restoration Adapter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.24006)**  `arXiv:2603.24006`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hongshen Zhao, Jingkang Tai, Yuhang Wu, Wenkang Zhang, Xi Lan, Shangyan Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Underwater video object segmentation (VOS) requires temporally consistent dense segmentation in scenes where water acts as a participating optical medium. Wavelength-dependent attenuation, scattering, turbidity, and unstable illumination alter object appearance, weaken mask-level localization cues, and destabilize temporal association, yet existing VOS benchmarks provide limited support for diagnosing these aquatic factors. We introduce UW-VOS, a large-scale diagnostic benchmark with 1,431 video sequences, 307,267 mask annotations, 411 semantic categories, and 16 fine-grained attributes. UW-VOS supports both standard VOS evaluation and factorized analysis of aquatic appearance degradation, spatial localization ambiguity, and temporal identity discontinuity. We further propose the Aquatic Restoration Adapter (ARA), a physics-derived parameter-efficient module that transfers the inverse structure of underwater image formation into bottleneck feature space and can be instantiated in different VOS backbones. Experiments show that representative open-air VOS methods suffer an average 13-point $\mathcal{J}\&\mathcal{F}$ drop on UW-VOS. ARA matches or surpasses full fine-tuning in both Transformer- and memory-based VOS backbones, and outperforms standard PEFT counterparts under a matched setting. These results establish UW-VOS as a diagnostic platform for underwater video perception and ARA as a compact feature-restoration module for aquatic domain adaptation. Project repository:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[SIGMA: A Physics-Based Benchmark for Gas Chimney Understanding in Seismic Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.23439)**  `arXiv:2603.23439`  `cs.CV`  
  _Bao Truong, Quang Nguyen, Baoru Huang, Jinpei Han, Van Nguyen, Ngan Le, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Seismic images reconstruct subsurface reflectivity from field recordings, guiding exploration and reservoir monitoring. Gas chimneys are vertical anomalies caused by subsurface fluid migration. Understanding these phenomena is crucial for assessing hydrocarbon potential and avoiding drilling hazards. However, accurate detection is challenging due to strong seismic attenuation and scattering. Traditional physics-based methods are computationally expensive and sensitive to model errors, while deep learning offers efficient alternatives, yet lacks labeled datasets. In this work, we introduce \textbf{SIGMA}, a new physics-based dataset for gas chimney understanding in seismic images, featuring (i) pixel-level gas-chimney mask for detection and (ii) paired degraded and ground-truth image for enhancement. We employed physics-based methods that cover a wide range of geological settings and data acquisition conditions. Comprehensive experiments demonstrate that SIGMA serves as a challenging benchmark for gas chimney interpretation and benefits general seismic understanding.
  </details>

- **[A Neuro-Symbolic Framework Combining Inductive and Deductive Reasoning for Autonomous Driving Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.12421)**  `arXiv:2603.12421`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hongyan Wei, Wael AbdAlmageed_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing end-to-end autonomous driving models rely heavily on purely data-driven inductive reasoning. This "black-box" nature limits interpretability and makes safety-critical planning challenging in complex, long-tail scenarios. To overcome this bottleneck, we propose a novel neuro-symbolic trajectory planning (NS-Plan) framework, which integrates rigorous deductive reasoning into end-to-end neural networks. Specifically, our framework utilizes a Large Language Model (LLM) to dynamically extract scene rules and employs an Answer Set Programming (ASP) solver for deterministic logical arbitration, generating logically consistent and traceable discrete driving decisions. To bridge the gap between discrete symbols and continuous trajectories, we introduce a decision-conditioned decoding mechanism that transforms high-level logical decisions into learnable embedding vectors, simultaneously constraining the planning query and the physical initial velocity of a differentiable Kinematic Bicycle Model (KBM). By combining KBM-generated baseline trajectories with neural residual corrections, our approach uses the KBM to guide trajectory generation while keeping the decision-making process traceable. On the nuScenes benchmark, our method (NS-Plan) comprehensively outperforms state-of-the-art baselines, reducing the average $L_2$ error by 18.3% (to 0.49 m), the collision rate by 22.2% (to 0.07%), and improving the trajectory prediction consistency (TPC) by 13% (to 0.47 m) compared to MomAD. More importantly, in the complex Turning-nuScenes subset, our method reduces the collision rate by 78.1% (to 0.07%) over the baseline.
  </details>

- **[Evaluation of Visual Processing Should Be Human-Centered, Not Metric-Centered](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00643)**  `arXiv:2603.00643`  `cs.CV`  
  _Fanghua Yu, Jinfan Hu, Zhiyuan You, Xiang Yin, Hongyu An, Xinqi Lin, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  This position paper argues that the evaluation of modern visual processing systems should no longer be driven primarily by single-metric image quality assessment benchmarks, particularly in the era of generative and perception-oriented methods. Image restoration exemplifies this divergence: while objective IQA metrics enable reproducible, scalable evaluation, they have increasingly drifted apart from human perception and user preferences. We contend that this mismatch risks constraining innovation and misguiding research progress across visual processing tasks. Rather than rejecting metrics altogether, this paper calls for a rebalancing of evaluation paradigms, advocating a more human-centered, context-aware, and fine-grained approach to assessing the visual models' outcomes.
  </details>

- **[UniDoc: Towards Unified Thinking in Documents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.12658)**  `arXiv:2512.12658`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qixin Xu, Haozhe Wang, Che Liu, Fangzhen Lin, Wenhu Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Answering questions over long, visually rich documents demands both cross-page structural reasoning and fine-grained visual understanding. Existing approaches sacrifice one for the other: retrieval-augmented methods fragment documents and lose inter-page structure; monolithic vision-language models preserve structure but scale poorly beyond a few pages. This impasse stems from a false premise: that the macroscopic task of evidence retrieval and the microscopic task of detailed reasoning must either be split into separate systems or be processed identically. By decoupling their perceptual resolutions rather than their architectures, a single model can achieve the structural holism of monolithic processing alongside the efficiency of targeted retrieval. UniDoc realizes this through two resolution-adaptive stages: a Fast Reading stage processes all pages at low resolution to structurally identify evidence pages, and a Focused Thinking stage re-processes only those pages at high resolution for multimodal inference. To train this pipeline, we construct 30K multi-hop reasoning instances with stage-level supervision. On MMLongBench-Doc, UniDoc yields relative accuracy gains of 55.7% on Qwen2.5-VL-7B and 16.9% on Qwen3-VL-8B, while significantly reducing token cost relative to uniform high-resolution processing. Error analysis identifies page retrieval and fine-grained visual reasoning as the remaining bottlenecks, delineating concrete targets for future work.
  </details>

- **[Dual-Granularity Semantic Prompting for Language Guidance Infrared Small Target Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.19306)**  `arXiv:2511.19306`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zixuan Wang, Haoran Sun, Jiaming Lu, Wenxuan Wang, Zhongling Huang, Dingwen Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Infrared small target detection remains challenging due to limited feature representation and severe background interference, resulting in sub-optimal performance. While recent CLIP-inspired methods attempt to leverage textual guidance for detection, they are hindered by inaccurate text descriptions and reliance on manual annotations. To overcome these limitations, we propose DGSPNet, an end-to-end language prompt-driven framework. Our approach integrates dual-granularity semantic prompts: coarse-grained textual priors (e.g., 'infrared image', 'small target') and fine-grained personalized semantic descriptions derived through visual-to-textual mapping within the image space. This design not only facilitates learning fine-grained semantic information but also can inherently leverage language prompts during inference without relying on any annotation requirements. By fully leveraging the precision and conciseness of text descriptions, we further introduce a text-guide channel attention (TGCA) mechanism and text-guide spatial attention (TGSA) mechanism that enhances the model's sensitivity to potential targets across both low- and high-level feature spaces. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method significantly improves detection accuracy and achieves state-of-the-art performance on three benchmark datasets.
  </details>

- **[Matching-Based Few-Shot Semantic Segmentation Models Are Interpretable by Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.18163)**  `arXiv:2511.18163`  `cs.CV`
